# **STEP-148 — Stability Certificate Consumer Acceptance / Cross-Consumer Currentness Reconciliation / Post-Certification Drift Invalidation and Safe-State Enforcement Contract**

STEP\_ID: 148  
CONTRACT\_ID: CHRON-STABILITY-CERT-CONSUMER-CURRENTNESS-148  
VERSION: 0.1.0-draft  
STATUS: DRAFT\_COMPLETE  
NORMATIVE\_STATUS: PROPOSED  
ADOPTION\_STATUS: NOT\_ADOPTED  
IMPLEMENTATION\_STATUS: NOT\_VERIFIED  
TEST\_STATUS: NOT\_EXECUTED  
REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION: NOT\_PERFORMED  
CANONICAL\_MUTATION: NONE  
FREEZE\_STATUS: NOT\_AUTHORIZED  
TIMEZONE: Asia/Tokyo

## **0\. Contract Position**

PREDECESSOR:  
  STEP-147  
  STEP-141..146  
  STEP-115..128  
  STEP-135..146

PRIMARY\_OBJECT:  
  Stability Certificate Consumer Currentness

SECONDARY\_OBJECTS:  
  Consumer Acceptance State  
  Cross-Consumer Reconciliation  
  Post-Certification Drift Invalidation  
  Downstream Authorization Impact  
  Safe-State Enforcement Evidence

AUTHORITY:  
  HISTORY \= CANONICAL TRUTH  
  STATE \= fold(HISTORY)  
  PROJECTION \= DERIVED  
  LLM \= OBSERVATION / PROPOSAL ONLY  
  KERNEL \= SOLE CANONICAL COMMIT AUTHORITY

EXECUTION:  
  DESIGN\_ONLY  
  NO\_REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION  
  NO\_IMPLEMENTATION  
  NO\_TEST\_EXECUTION  
  NO\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION

STEP-148 defines the conditions under which a consumer may claim that a Stability Certificate has been received, validated, accepted, and remains current. It also defines how cross-consumer discrepancies, post-certification drift, certificate invalidation, downstream authorization consequences, and Safe-State enforcement are reconciled.

The contract must not collapse independent consumer states into a single `CURRENT` Boolean. A certificate may remain valid in the canonical registry while one or more consumers hold stale copies, have not acknowledged invalidation, or retain downstream authorization derived from an earlier certificate revision.

Core requirement: certificate currentness is an evidence-backed, version-bound, consumer-specific determination. It is not inferred from publication success, registry state, elapsed time, missing errors, or the absence of a consumer report.

## **1\. Purpose**

1. Establish a deterministic consumer acceptance lifecycle for Stability Certificates issued under STEP-147.  
2. Bind every consumer decision to an exact certificate identity, revision, digest, scope, baseline, policy, and evidence manifest.  
3. Reconcile registry state against consumer-reported state without treating missing reports as positive evidence.  
4. Identify and propagate post-certification drift, invalidation, revocation, supersession, and re-certification.  
5. Track the downstream authorization consequences of certificate state changes.  
6. Separate invalidation intent, canonical invalidation, propagation, consumer effect, and global closure.  
7. Require independently verifiable evidence for Safe-State requests, execution, and effect verification.  
8. Produce a cross-consumer currentness assessment with explicit unresolved and unknown states.  
9. Preserve event history and replay determinism without re-executing external actions.  
10. Prevent certificate currentness from implicitly granting operational re-entry authorization or Freeze authority.

## **2\. Non-Goals**

STEP-148 does not authorize:

* issuing or modifying a Stability Certificate;  
* performing the STEP-147 stability assessment;  
* publishing a certificate or changing the canonical registry;  
* executing production operations, stopping workloads, or changing external systems;  
* revoking downstream authorization without the applicable authorization and enforcement contracts;  
* declaring global convergence from a subset of consumer reports;  
* treating Safe-State execution as proof that the intended effect occurred;  
* authorizing operational re-entry;  
* authorizing normative adoption, production deployment, or Freeze;  
* repairing malformed evidence or filling unknown fields by inference.

These actions remain governed by their corresponding contracts and explicit authorization gates.

## **3\. Normative Language and Decision Discipline**

`MUST`, `MUST NOT`, `SHOULD`, and `MAY` indicate normative strength within this proposed contract. They do not, by themselves, establish adoption.

Every decision must preserve these separate stages:

OBSERVATION  
  \-\> PROPOSAL  
  \-\> STRUCTURAL\_VALIDATION  
  \-\> AUTHORITY\_VALIDATION  
  \-\> POLICY\_EVALUATION  
  \-\> AUTHORIZATION  
  \-\> CANONICAL\_COMMIT  
  \-\> EXTERNAL\_EFFECT  
  \-\> EFFECT\_VERIFICATION  
  \-\> RECONCILIATION

A stage may be omitted only where the governing contract explicitly defines that omission as valid. A later-stage state must never be inferred solely from an earlier-stage success.

## **4\. Normative State Distinctions**

The following equivalences are prohibited.

STABILITY\_CERTIFICATE\_ISSUED \!= CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED  
CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED \!= CONSUMER\_RECEIVED  
CONSUMER\_RECEIVED \!= CONSUMER\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
CONSUMER\_ACKNOWLEDGED \!= CONSUMER\_VALIDATED  
CONSUMER\_VALIDATED \!= CONSUMER\_ACCEPTED  
CONSUMER\_ACCEPTED \!= CONSUMER\_CURRENT

REGISTRY\_CURRENT \!= ALL\_CONSUMERS\_CURRENT  
INVALIDATION\_REQUESTED \!= INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED \!= INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED \!= ALL\_CONSUMERS\_INVALIDATED

ACK\_MISSING \!= DELIVERY\_FAILED  
ACK\_MISSING \!= CONSUMER\_CURRENT  
TIMEOUT \!= SUCCESS  
TIMEOUT \!= FAILURE

DRIFT\_DETECTED \!= DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED \!= IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_STOPPED

SAFE\_STATE\_REQUESTED \!= SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTED  
SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTED \!= SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

CROSS\_CONSUMER\_CURRENTNESS\_ASSESSED \!= CROSS\_CONSUMER\_CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFIED  
STABILITY\_CERTIFIED \!= OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

REPLAY \!= REDELIVERY  
REPLAY \!= CANONICAL\_MUTATION  
COMMIT\_REQUESTED \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED  
COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING \!= COMMIT\_FAILED

Implementations MUST represent these distinctions in their state model, event records, and validation rules. A presentation layer MAY summarize states, but MUST NOT erase distinctions needed for authorization, audit, or recovery.

## **5\. Terminology**

| Term | Normative meaning |
| ----- | ----- |
| Stability Certificate | STEP-147-issued artifact representing a stability assessment and its bounded validity conditions. |
| Certificate Revision | Monotonically ordered revision of a certificate identity within its defined revision domain. |
| Certificate Digest | Cryptographic digest of the specified canonical certificate representation. |
| Consumer | A registered subsystem, service, controller, or process that consumes a certificate or a decision derived from it. |
| Consumer Epoch | Versioned identity of a consumer incarnation or state domain, preventing reports from an earlier incarnation from being mistaken for current state. |
| Consumer Acceptance | Consumer-local decision that a particular certificate revision satisfies the consumer's configured acceptance policy. |
| Consumer Currentness | Determination that the consumer's accepted state remains consistent with current canonical certificate, scope, baseline, policy, and relevant dependency state. |
| Consumer Cohort | Versioned set of consumers classified as required, optional, or excluded for a particular reconciliation assessment. |
| Publication Event | Durable event recording the publication intent or result according to the applicable publication contract. |
| Invalidation Event | Durable record of a committed decision that a certificate or its permitted use is no longer valid under specified conditions. |
| Drift | Evidence-backed deviation from the assumptions, scope, baseline, policy, dependencies, or validity conditions of a certificate. |
| Downstream Authorization | Permission, permit, lease, or decision whose validity depends on a certificate or its acceptance state. |
| Safe-State Enforcement | Controlled action intended to reduce risk by restricting or changing a consumer's operational state, with execution and effect evidence tracked separately. |
| Reconciliation | Comparison of independently recorded canonical and consumer states, producing explicit matches, discrepancies, and unresolved items. |
| Currentness Assessment | A version-bound evaluation of consumer currentness across a frozen cohort. |
| Currentness Certification | A separately authorized, committed determination that the assessment satisfies the defined closure policy. |

A consumer is identified by a stable `consumer_id` and a scoped `consumer_epoch`. Consumer identity reuse MUST NOT make evidence from a previous epoch valid for a new incarnation.

## **6\. Canonical Authority and Trust Boundaries**

### **6.1 Canonical authority**

The Kernel remains the sole canonical commit authority. The canonical History records certificate lifecycle changes, invalidation decisions, cohort revisions, reconciliation decisions, and any authorized enforcement outcomes that are within the Kernel's commit domain.

History  
  \-\> deterministic state projection  
  \-\> reconciliation input snapshot  
  \-\> validation  
  \-\> policy decision  
  \-\> authorized proposal  
  \-\> Kernel commit  
  \-\> derived registry and consumer views

Consumer reports are evidence. They are not authoritative mutations of the certificate registry.

### **6.2 Consumer authority boundaries**

A consumer MAY authoritatively report its own locally observed state only within its registered reporting authority. Such a report does not, by itself, establish that the canonical certificate remains valid or that all other consumers have converged.

The consumer's self-reported `CURRENT` state MUST be independently checked against:

* the current canonical certificate lifecycle state;  
* the consumer's exact accepted certificate revision and digest;  
* the active cohort snapshot;  
* the current baseline, scope, and applicable policies;  
* all relevant committed invalidation, supersession, and drift events;  
* the consumer's downstream dependency and authorization state;  
* the freshness and trust requirements for the report.

### **6.3 LLM boundary**

LLM\_OUTPUT \= OBSERVATION\_OR\_PROPOSAL  
LLM\_OUTPUT \!= CANONICAL\_STATE  
LLM\_OUTPUT \!= ACCEPTANCE\_AUTHORITY  
LLM\_OUTPUT \!= INVALIDATION\_COMMIT  
LLM\_OUTPUT \!= SAFE\_STATE\_AUTHORIZATION  
LLM\_OUTPUT \!= CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATION

LLM-generated candidate classifications MAY support review. They MUST NOT independently change canonical state or convert missing evidence into a successful decision.

## **7\. Required Input Contract**

Every cross-consumer assessment MUST bind to an immutable input snapshot or an equivalently verifiable version boundary.

### **7.1 Required inputs**

assessment\_input:  
  assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_revision: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  registry\_snapshot\_id: REQUIRED  
  registry\_revision: REQUIRED  
  registry\_digest: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_id: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_revision: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_digest: REQUIRED  
  stability\_window\_id: REQUIRED  
  stability\_assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_cohort\_id: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_cohort\_revision: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_cohort\_digest: REQUIRED  
  invalidation\_ledger\_revision: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_id: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_revision: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_digest: REQUIRED  
  reconciliation\_cutoff: REQUIRED  
  assessor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  input\_integrity\_evidence: REQUIRED

The exact identifiers and digest algorithms MUST be specified by the governing schemas and trust policy. This contract does not silently select a cryptographic algorithm, serialization format, or timestamp authority.

### **7.2 Input acceptance states**

Each required input MUST be classified as one of:

VALID  
INVALID  
INCOMPLETE  
STALE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN  
UNAVAILABLE

`INVALID` inputs prohibit the affected assessment from proceeding. `INCOMPLETE`, `STALE`, `CONFLICTED`, `UNKNOWN`, and `UNAVAILABLE` MUST NOT be coerced into `VALID`.

An assessment MAY record a blocked or diagnostic result when inputs are not valid, but MUST NOT produce a successful currentness certification from such inputs.

### **7.3 Snapshot consistency**

The input set MUST be internally consistent at the selected reconciliation boundary. If the registry, cohort, invalidation ledger, or dependency snapshot changes during evaluation, the implementation MUST either:

1. evaluate against the pinned snapshot and explicitly state its cutoff; or  
2. abort the current attempt and construct a new version-bound assessment.

The system MUST NOT silently mix revisions from incompatible snapshots.

## **8\. Stability Certificate Consumer Acceptance Record**

Every consumer acceptance report MUST bind to the exact object and context being accepted.

consumer\_acceptance\_record:  
  record\_id: REQUIRED  
  record\_revision: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_id: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_epoch: REQUIRED

  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED

  baseline\_id: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_revision: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_digest: REQUIRED

  stability\_assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  stability\_window\_id: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED

  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  policy\_digest: REQUIRED

  publication\_id: REQUIRED  
  publication\_revision: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_id: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_revision: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_digest: REQUIRED

  received\_at: OPTIONAL  
  acknowledged\_at: OPTIONAL  
  validated\_at: OPTIONAL  
  accepted\_at: OPTIONAL  
  last\_reconciled\_at: OPTIONAL

  reported\_state: REQUIRED  
  observed\_registry\_revision: REQUIRED  
  observed\_invalidation\_revision: REQUIRED  
  observed\_dependency\_revision: REQUIRED

  downstream\_dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  downstream\_authorization\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  report\_integrity\_proof: REQUIRED  
  report\_provenance: REQUIRED

### **8.1 Binding requirements**

* The certificate identity MUST include enough information to distinguish the certificate from other certificates and revisions.  
* A digest MUST be checked against the agreed canonical representation; a matching identifier alone is insufficient.  
* The consumer epoch MUST match the epoch authorized to report for the relevant cohort revision.  
* The accepted scope MUST not exceed the certificate's authorized scope.  
* Policy revisions MUST be explicit. An acceptance under a superseded policy MUST be re-evaluated when that policy is mandatory for currentness.  
* Downstream dependency references MUST be enumerable or explicitly marked as incomplete under a versioned completeness policy.  
* Optional timestamps MUST NOT be used as substitutes for missing state-transition evidence.  
* Evidence manifests MUST reference verifiable evidence, not merely list unverified filenames or assertions.

### **8.2 Consumer report rejection**

A report MUST be rejected or placed into a non-authoritative diagnostic state when it has:

* a mismatched certificate digest or revision;  
* an invalid or untrusted report integrity proof;  
* an unauthorized consumer identity or epoch;  
* an unrecognized schema or prohibited revision regression;  
* an incompatible scope, baseline, or policy binding;  
* conflicting state-transition evidence;  
* an invalid provenance chain;  
* missing required evidence;  
* a duplicate identity with non-identical content where idempotency requires exact equivalence.

Rejection MUST preserve the submitted report's audit provenance. Rejection MUST NOT overwrite the last known valid canonical or consumer state.

## **9\. Consumer Lifecycle State Machine**

### **9.1 Required states**

NOT\_SCHEDULED  
QUEUED  
DELIVERY\_PENDING  
DELIVERED  
RECEIVED  
ACKNOWLEDGED  
VALIDATION\_PENDING  
VALIDATED  
ACCEPTANCE\_PENDING  
ACCEPTED  
CURRENT  
STALE  
INVALIDATION\_PENDING  
INVALIDATED  
SUPERSEDED  
REJECTED  
UNKNOWN  
RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
RETRY\_PENDING  
EXHAUSTED

`CURRENT` is a derived or committed state only under the applicable authority model; it MUST NOT be inferred solely from a consumer-provided label.

### **9.2 Transition conditions**

| Transition | Minimum evidence |
| ----- | ----- |
| `NOT_SCHEDULED → QUEUED` | Consumer is included in a valid cohort revision and delivery is authorized. |
| `QUEUED → DELIVERY_PENDING` | Durable dispatch intent or equivalent delivery ledger entry. |
| `DELIVERY_PENDING → DELIVERED` | Delivery evidence under the publication transport contract. |
| `DELIVERED → RECEIVED` | Consumer-side receipt evidence bound to the publication identity. |
| `RECEIVED → ACKNOWLEDGED` | Authenticated acknowledgment bound to the exact publication and certificate revision. |
| `ACKNOWLEDGED → VALIDATION_PENDING` | Intake record accepted for validation processing. |
| `VALIDATION_PENDING → VALIDATED` | Signature, digest, schema, scope, version, provenance, and policy checks completed. |
| `VALIDATED → ACCEPTANCE_PENDING` | Consumer-specific acceptance policy evaluation initiated. |
| `ACCEPTANCE_PENDING → ACCEPTED` | Acceptance decision and required evidence committed under the applicable authority model. |
| `ACCEPTED → CURRENT` | Reconciliation proves currentness at a defined snapshot and cutoff. |
| `CURRENT → STALE` | A relevant state change makes the previous currentness determination obsolete or unproven. |
| Any eligible state `→ INVALIDATION_PENDING` | A relevant invalidation or mandatory revalidation event is observed. |
| `INVALIDATION_PENDING → INVALIDATED` | The required invalidation effect is verified for that consumer and target. |
| Any eligible state `→ SUPERSEDED` | A newer valid certificate revision replaces the referenced revision for the relevant scope. |
| Any non-terminal processing state `→ RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | Canonical and reported state cannot be reconciled using current evidence. |
| `RECONCILIATION_PENDING → CURRENT` | A fresh, complete, authorized reconciliation establishes currentness. |
| `RECONCILIATION_PENDING → UNKNOWN` | The available evidence cannot determine the consumer's state. |
| `RETRY_PENDING → EXHAUSTED` | The applicable retry policy has been exhausted and the outcome remains unresolved. |

A state transition MUST have a defined trigger, required evidence, authorization requirement where applicable, and durable outcome record.

### **9.3 Terminality and recovery**

`REJECTED`, `INVALIDATED`, `SUPERSEDED`, and `EXHAUSTED` MUST NOT be treated as interchangeable.

A rejected report may be resubmitted as a new report. An invalidated certificate may require a replacement certificate and fresh acceptance. A superseded certificate may remain historically valid for its original period while no longer being current. An exhausted delivery attempt may be retried under a new authorized attempt.

Any transition out of these states MUST be explicit and governed by policy. No implicit transition back to `CURRENT` is permitted.

## **10\. Consumer Cohort Governance**

### **10.1 Cohort classification**

Every currentness assessment MUST use a versioned, immutable cohort snapshot.

consumer\_cohort:  
  cohort\_id: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_revision: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_digest: REQUIRED  
  membership\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  membership\_policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  snapshot\_cutoff: REQUIRED  
  consumers:  
    \- consumer\_id: REQUIRED  
      consumer\_epoch: REQUIRED  
      membership\_class: REQUIRED  
      scope\_binding: REQUIRED  
      dependency\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
      acceptance\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED

Allowed membership classes:

REQUIRED  
OPTIONAL  
EXCLUDED

Membership MUST be determined under an explicit membership policy. An implementation MUST NOT silently classify an unreachable consumer as optional or excluded to obtain a passing result.

### **10.2 Required consumers**

A `REQUIRED` consumer MUST have a resolved currentness outcome satisfying the assessment policy before global currentness certification can pass.

The following states are insufficient:

UNKNOWN  
STALE  
RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
INVALIDATION\_PENDING  
RETRY\_PENDING  
EXHAUSTED

A policy MAY define a restricted or degraded outcome for specified consumers, but such an outcome MUST be represented as a separate decision class. It MUST NOT be relabeled as full currentness.

### **10.3 Optional consumers**

An `OPTIONAL` consumer may be excluded from the mandatory pass predicate only when the policy explicitly allows it. Its unresolved state MUST remain visible in the assessment.

The existence of optional consumers does not authorize the omission of their known safety-relevant dependencies from drift or downstream-impact analysis.

### **10.4 Excluded consumers**

Every `EXCLUDED` consumer MUST have an auditable exclusion reason, authority, scope, effective revision, and policy reference.

Exclusion MUST NOT erase historical acceptance, delivery, invalidation, or operation records. If a previously required consumer becomes excluded, the cohort change MUST be recorded as a new revision; it MUST NOT retroactively rewrite a prior assessment.

### **10.5 Cohort changes during assessment**

If membership changes after the snapshot cutoff:

* the running assessment remains bound to its original cohort;  
* the new membership revision MUST be evaluated separately or through an explicitly authorized restart;  
* no assessment may claim coverage of the new cohort using evidence from the old cohort without verifying compatibility;  
* a newly required consumer MUST block certification until its required state is resolved.

## **11\. Certificate Currentness Evaluation**

### **11.1 Currentness predicate**

A consumer MAY be classified `CURRENT` only when all mandatory conditions are satisfied:

CONSUMER\_CURRENT(c, snapshot) :=  
    IDENTITY\_VALID(c)  
AND EPOCH\_VALID(c)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_ID\_MATCH(c)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_REVISION\_MATCH(c)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_DIGEST\_MATCH(c)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_CANONICALLY\_VALID(snapshot)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_NOT\_REVOKED(snapshot)  
AND CERTIFICATE\_NOT\_SUPERSEDED\_FOR\_SCOPE(snapshot)  
AND SCOPE\_COMPATIBLE(c, snapshot)  
AND BASELINE\_CURRENT(c, snapshot)  
AND POLICY\_CURRENT(c, snapshot)  
AND EVIDENCE\_MANIFEST\_ACCEPTED(c, snapshot)  
AND DEPENDENCIES\_CURRENT(c, snapshot)  
AND INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_CLEAR(c, snapshot)  
AND REPORT\_TRUST\_VALID(c)  
AND REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE\_COMPLETE(c)  
AND NO\_UNRESOLVED\_CONFLICT(c, snapshot)

The implementation MUST define the precise evaluation semantics for every predicate. Missing predicate inputs produce `UNKNOWN` or a more specific failure classification; they MUST NOT default to `true`.

### **11.2 Currentness is time- and revision-bounded**

A currentness result MUST bind to:

* the registry snapshot and revision;  
* the certificate revision and digest;  
* the cohort snapshot and digest;  
* the invalidation ledger revision;  
* the dependency snapshot;  
* the relevant policy revisions;  
* the reconciliation cutoff.

The result MUST NOT be treated as an eternal property of a consumer. A subsequent relevant event may immediately make the prior determination stale or trigger invalidation.

### **11.3 Certificate validity versus consumer currentness**

The assessment MUST distinguish:

1. Whether the certificate was validly issued.  
2. Whether the certificate is currently valid in the canonical registry.  
3. Whether a particular consumer received and accepted the correct revision.  
4. Whether the consumer remains current against relevant changes.  
5. Whether the consumer's dependent authorization remains valid.  
6. Whether the associated operational activity remains permitted.

A positive answer to one question does not establish a positive answer to the next.

### **11.4 Cross-consumer assessment result**

Allowed assessment outcomes:

PASS  
PASS\_WITH\_CONSTRAINTS  
FAIL  
UNKNOWN  
CONFLICTED  
EVIDENCE\_INSUFFICIENT  
INVALIDATED  
SUPERSEDED  
BLOCKED

`PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS` is permitted only when the assessment policy defines the allowable constraints, affected consumers, risk bounds, expiry conditions, and required authority. Otherwise, unresolved required-consumer states prohibit a passing result.

`FAIL`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONFLICTED`, `EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT`, `INVALIDATED`, `SUPERSEDED`, and `BLOCKED` MUST remain distinguishable in records and downstream decisions.

## **12\. Publication and Consumer State Reconciliation**

STEP-141 defines certificate publication and distribution concerns; STEP-142 defines consumer-side intake; STEP-143 defines consumer state reporting and convergence evidence. STEP-148 consumes their records to determine currentness. It MUST NOT silently redefine their authority or erase their separate state transitions.

### **12.1 Reconciliation inputs**

For each cohort member, compare:

CANONICAL\_CERTIFICATE\_STATE  
PUBLICATION\_LEDGER\_STATE  
DELIVERY\_LEDGER\_STATE  
CONSUMER\_REPORTED\_STATE  
CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE\_RECORD  
INVALIDATION\_LEDGER\_STATE  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_STATE  
CONSUMER\_DEPENDENCY\_STATE  
SAFE\_STATE\_ENFORCEMENT\_STATE

Each comparison result MUST be one of:

MATCH  
MISMATCH  
MISSING  
STALE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN  
NOT\_APPLICABLE

`NOT_APPLICABLE` requires a policy-grounded reason. It MUST NOT serve as a generic replacement for unavailable evidence.

### **12.2 Missing acknowledgment**

An absent acknowledgment establishes only that a qualifying acknowledgment was not observed within the relevant evidence boundary.

It does not independently establish:

* that delivery failed;  
* that delivery succeeded;  
* that the consumer received the certificate;  
* that the consumer accepted the certificate;  
* that the consumer is current;  
* that the consumer has invalidated a certificate;  
* that a downstream operation has stopped.

The system MUST retain the last verified state and mark its currentness as unresolved or stale when the applicable policy requires it.

### **12.3 Delayed, duplicate, and reordered reports**

Reports MUST be correlated by durable identity and version. Duplicate reports MUST be idempotently processed according to a defined identity rule. Reordered reports MUST NOT regress a consumer to an older accepted revision.

A delayed report that is internally valid but references an obsolete certificate MUST be preserved as historical evidence and MUST NOT restore currentness.

### **12.4 Registry-consumer disagreement**

When canonical registry state and consumer state disagree:

1. preserve both records and their provenance;  
2. identify the exact revision, digest, epoch, and cutoff of each;  
3. determine whether the disagreement is explained by event ordering or is an actual inconsistency;  
4. mark the affected consumer `RECONCILIATION_PENDING`, `STALE`, or `UNKNOWN` as warranted;  
5. apply the applicable safety and authorization policy;  
6. reconcile through an authorized, durable transition;  
7. record evidence of the resolved state.

A reconciliation algorithm MUST NOT choose the consumer's value over canonical History merely because the consumer report is newer by wall-clock timestamp.

## **13\. Post-Certification Drift Detection**

### **13.1 Drift trigger classes**

Drift MUST be evaluated against the certificate's explicit scope, baseline, validity conditions, and dependency profile.

Recognized trigger classes include:

RUNTIME\_DRIFT  
CONFIGURATION\_DRIFT  
CAPABILITY\_DRIFT  
DEPENDENCY\_DRIFT  
POLICY\_DRIFT  
BASELINE\_DRIFT  
SCOPE\_DRIFT  
EVIDENCE\_INVALIDATION  
EVIDENCE\_TRUST\_REVOCATION  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION  
CERTIFICATE\_SUPERSESSION  
CONSUMER\_EPOCH\_CHANGE  
CONSUMER\_MEMBERSHIP\_CHANGE  
AUTHORIZATION\_STATE\_DRIFT  
DOWNSTREAM\_OPERATION\_DRIFT  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_DRIFT  
INTEGRITY\_KEY\_REVOCATION  
CLOCK\_OR\_FRESHNESS\_UNCERTAINTY  
UNKNOWN\_DRIFT  
CONFLICTED\_DRIFT

These classes are descriptive categories, not a claim that all triggers have identical consequences. The impact of each trigger MUST be determined by a versioned impact policy.

### **13.2 Drift record**

drift\_record:  
  drift\_id: REQUIRED  
  drift\_revision: REQUIRED  
  detected\_at: REQUIRED  
  detector\_identity: REQUIRED  
  detector\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  detector\_policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  trigger\_class: REQUIRED  
  affected\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  affected\_certificate\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_consumer\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  confidence\_or\_certainty\_class: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_status: REQUIRED  
  resolution\_status: REQUIRED

The schema MUST define whether a given certainty field is numeric, categorical, or both. An uncalibrated model confidence score MUST NOT substitute for validated evidence.

### **13.3 Detection versus adjudication**

DRIFT\_OBSERVED  
  \!= DRIFT\_VALIDATED  
  \!= IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_PROPOSED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_AUTHORIZED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED

A detector may report a candidate trigger without establishing that a certificate is invalid. However, where the safety policy requires precautionary restriction upon a credible candidate, the restriction MUST follow its own authorization and enforcement contract.

### **13.4 Drift impact closure**

For every validated drift event, the system MUST establish:

* affected certificate identities and revisions;  
* affected scopes and consumers;  
* dependent authorizations, permits, leases, and operations;  
* required revalidation or invalidation actions;  
* applicable deadlines and retry policy;  
* evidence needed to verify each required effect;  
* the responsible authority and unresolved risks.

If the impact set cannot be determined completely, the record MUST explicitly represent the impact as incomplete or unknown. The system MUST NOT assert that an empty impact set has been proven when dependency discovery failed.

## **14\. Invalidation, Revocation, and Supersession Propagation**

### **14.1 Separate lifecycle stages**

The invalidation lifecycle MUST distinguish:

INVALIDATION\_DETECTED  
INVALIDATION\_PROPOSED  
INVALIDATION\_AUTHORIZED  
INVALIDATION\_COMMIT\_PENDING  
INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
INVALIDATION\_PUBLISHED  
INVALIDATION\_DELIVERY\_PENDING  
INVALIDATION\_RECEIVED  
INVALIDATION\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
INVALIDATION\_RECONCILED

Not every intermediate state necessarily occurs in every transport or consumer architecture. Any omitted stage MUST be justified by the governing contract, not by implementation convenience.

`INVALIDATION_COMMITTED` is a canonical decision. It does not prove that consumers have received the decision or enforced its consequences.

### **14.2 Durable event ledger**

Every committed invalidation MUST be represented by a durable event that identifies:

* the affected certificate and revision;  
* the invalidation reason and policy;  
* the effective scope;  
* the authority and authorization evidence;  
* the canonical commit identity;  
* the event revision and ordering information;  
* affected cohort and dependency snapshots;  
* required consumer acknowledgments and effect evidence;  
* superseding or replacement certificate references, if any;  
* unresolved propagation and operation-reconciliation obligations.

An invalidation event MUST NOT be silently deleted or rewritten to make a propagation failure disappear.

### **14.3 Partial publication or delivery**

If invalidation propagation partially completes, the system MUST:

1. retain the committed invalidation as canonical truth;  
2. retain per-consumer delivery and effect states;  
3. mark unresolved consumers explicitly;  
4. prevent the overall propagation process from being reported as complete;  
5. retry or reconcile according to an authorized policy;  
6. preserve evidence of duplicate delivery, delayed delivery, and conflicting reports.

The design SHOULD use a durable outbox/inbox or an equivalent mechanism that supports recovery from partial failure. The chosen mechanism and its atomicity guarantees remain implementation-specific and MUST be proven rather than assumed.

### **14.4 Idempotency and ordering**

Every propagation attempt MUST have a stable identity or a deterministically derived idempotency key under an explicit rule.

* Repeated processing of the same event MUST NOT duplicate canonical invalidation effects.  
* Older invalidation events MUST NOT be discarded merely because a newer event exists.  
* A replacement certificate MUST NOT erase obligations arising from the previous certificate's invalidation.  
* A consumer MUST NOT accept a superseded revision as current solely because it was delivered after the replacement.  
* Retries MUST NOT silently produce new canonical decisions.

### **14.5 Invalidation effect verification**

The required effect is consumer-specific. It may include marking a local certificate invalid, refusing new authorization decisions, revoking a local permit, or reconciling a dependent operation.

The contract MUST define the expected effect for each consumer class. A generic acknowledgment is insufficient if the policy requires proof that a specific downstream gate changed state.

Global invalidation closure requires all mandatory effects to be verified under the applicable cohort and impact policy. Unresolved optional consumers and residual operations MUST remain visible even when policy permits a constrained result.

## **15\. Downstream Authorization and In-Flight Operation Control**

### **15.1 Dependency binding**

Every downstream authorization derived from a Stability Certificate MUST identify its certificate dependency and applicable validity conditions.

downstream\_authorization\_dependency:  
  authorization\_id: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_revision: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_id: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_epoch: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  validity\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_state: REQUIRED  
  in\_flight\_operation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  reconciliation\_state: REQUIRED

If an authorization depends on multiple certificates or other evidence, the dependency set MUST be complete under a versioned dependency-discovery policy. Incomplete dependency knowledge MUST remain explicit.

### **15.2 Authorization state separation**

AUTHORIZATION\_ACTIVE  
AUTHORIZATION\_STALE  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVOCATION\_PENDING  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED  
AUTHORIZATION\_EXPIRY\_PENDING  
AUTHORIZATION\_EXPIRED  
AUTHORIZATION\_UNKNOWN

A detected drift MUST trigger the policy-defined authorization impact evaluation. It MUST NOT be assumed that every drift automatically revokes every authorization, nor may a potentially affected authorization remain implicitly valid because impact analysis has not completed.

### **15.3 New operation fencing**

Where the applicable safety policy requires restrictions, the enforcement mechanism MUST prevent new operations from starting under a known invalid, stale, revoked, or unresolved authorization.

A successful authorization check MUST be bound to the actual execution boundary. A check performed earlier in the workflow is insufficient if the relevant certificate or authorization state can change before the operation starts.

The precise fencing, lease, version-check, or atomic execution mechanism remains an implementation decision. Its correctness MUST be demonstrated under the applicable concurrency and failure model.

### **15.4 In-flight operations**

Revoking an authorization does not establish that an already-running operation has stopped.

Every affected in-flight operation MUST be classified according to evidence, for example:

NOT\_STARTED  
START\_PENDING  
RUNNING  
STOP\_REQUESTED  
STOP\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
STOPPED  
EFFECTS\_RECONCILED  
COMPENSATION\_PENDING  
COMPENSATED  
UNKNOWN

The governing policy MUST determine whether to stop, pause, compensate, isolate, or allow an operation to finish under constrained conditions.

The reconciliation record MUST include:

* the operation identity and last verified execution state;  
* the authorization and certificate revision used at its execution boundary;  
* the detected drift or invalidation event;  
* the applicable control decision and authorization;  
* the external command or control request, if any;  
* acknowledgment and effect-verification evidence;  
* residual side effects, compensation requirements, and unresolved risk.

If the operation's state is unknown, the system MUST NOT report it as stopped or safely completed.

### **15.5 Authorization closure**

A downstream authorization is reconciled only when the required authorization state and its dependent operation consequences have been verified.

AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED  
  \!= IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_STOPPED  
  \!= EXTERNAL\_EFFECTS\_RECONCILED  
  \!= RESIDUAL\_RISK\_CLOSED

The authorization ledger and operation ledger MUST remain independently auditable.

## **16\. Safe-State Enforcement Contract**

### **16.1 Safe-State trigger**

Safe-State enforcement may be required when drift, invalidation, consumer inconsistency, or downstream authorization uncertainty crosses a policy-defined threshold.

The trigger MUST identify:

* the triggering event and evidence;  
* affected consumers, targets, scopes, and operations;  
* the relevant risk and impact policy revisions;  
* the requested Safe-State profile;  
* the required authorization;  
* the expected effects and verification conditions;  
* the fallback action if enforcement fails or remains unknown.

A trigger does not itself authorize arbitrary external action. The system MUST apply the designated emergency or enforcement authority contract.

### **16.2 Safe-State lifecycle**

SAFE\_STATE\_TRIGGERED  
SAFE\_STATE\_PROPOSED  
SAFE\_STATE\_VALIDATION\_PENDING  
SAFE\_STATE\_VALIDATED  
SAFE\_STATE\_AUTHORIZATION\_PENDING  
SAFE\_STATE\_AUTHORIZED  
SAFE\_STATE\_REQUESTED  
SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTION\_PENDING  
SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTED  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
SAFE\_STATE\_RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
SAFE\_STATE\_RECONCILED  
SAFE\_STATE\_FAILED  
SAFE\_STATE\_UNKNOWN

`SAFE_STATE_EXECUTED` requires execution evidence. `SAFE_STATE_EFFECT_VERIFIED` requires independent or policy-approved evidence of the intended effect. `SAFE_STATE_RECONCILED` requires closure of all required target-level obligations.

### **16.3 Target-level enforcement evidence**

Every enforcement target MUST have an independently traceable outcome.

safe\_state\_target\_record:  
  target\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_revision: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_id: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_epoch: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  safe\_state\_profile\_id: REQUIRED  
  safe\_state\_profile\_revision: REQUIRED  
  trigger\_event\_refs: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  request\_id: REQUIRED  
  execution\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  effect\_verification\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  observed\_post\_action\_state: REQUIRED  
  residual\_operation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  reconciliation\_status: REQUIRED

If an action affects multiple targets, a success response for one target MUST NOT be interpreted as success for all targets.

### **16.4 Safe-State failure**

If execution fails, times out, or produces an unknown result:

* preserve the original request and execution evidence;  
* retain the last verified target state;  
* mark the affected target as failed or unknown according to evidence;  
* execute only policy-authorized retries or fallback actions;  
* continue tracking in-flight operations and external effects;  
* block any dependent clearance whose prerequisites are not proven.

A timeout MUST NOT be automatically interpreted as failure, success, or proof that no side effect occurred.

### **16.5 Safe-State is not re-entry**

A verified Safe-State transition does not automatically authorize recovery, normal operation, certificate reissuance, or operational re-entry.

Re-entry requires its own applicable contract, fresh evidence, authorization, and execution-boundary controls.

## **17\. Cross-Consumer Currentness Assessment and Certification**

### **17.1 Assessment output**

cross\_consumer\_currentness\_assessment:  
  assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_revision: REQUIRED  
  outcome: REQUIRED

  registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invalidation\_ledger\_ref: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED

  reconciliation\_cutoff: REQUIRED  
  required\_consumer\_count: REQUIRED  
  resolved\_required\_consumer\_count: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_required\_consumer\_refs: REQUIRED  
  optional\_consumer\_exceptions: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_consumer\_records: REQUIRED

  drift\_records: REQUIRED  
  invalidation\_propagation\_records: REQUIRED  
  downstream\_authorization\_reconciliation: REQUIRED  
  safe\_state\_enforcement\_reconciliation: REQUIRED

  residual\_inconsistency\_manifest: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  assessor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_integrity\_proof: REQUIRED

Counts MUST be derived from the frozen cohort snapshot and the assessment's explicit state-classification rules. They MUST NOT be supplied as unsupported assertions.

### **17.2 Assessment versus certification**

ASSESSMENT\_REQUESTED  
  \!= ASSESSMENT\_COMPLETED  
  \!= ASSESSMENT\_PASSED  
  \!= CERTIFICATION\_AUTHORIZED  
  \!= CERTIFICATION\_COMMITTED  
  \!= CERTIFICATION\_CURRENT

The assessment is a determination supported by evidence. Certification is a separate governance transition requiring the applicable authority, validation, and commit conditions.

### **17.3 Certification pass conditions**

A cross-consumer currentness certification MAY pass only if all applicable conditions hold:

1. All required inputs are valid and bound to consistent snapshots.  
2. The referenced Stability Certificate is canonically valid for the assessed scope and cutoff.  
3. Certificate identity, revision, and digest are verified.  
4. The cohort snapshot is complete and its membership policy is valid.  
5. Every required consumer has a resolved, evidence-backed currentness outcome.  
6. No required consumer remains unknown, stale, conflicted, or pending mandatory reconciliation.  
7. Relevant invalidation and supersession events have been accounted for.  
8. Required invalidation effects have been verified at affected consumers.  
9. Downstream authorization impacts have been reconciled to the policy-required degree.  
10. In-flight operations have been accounted for; unknown effects are not treated as closed.  
11. Required Safe-State enforcement effects have been verified.  
12. The residual inconsistency manifest is complete and satisfies the applicable policy.  
13. Evidence provenance, integrity, freshness, and completeness checks pass.  
14. The assessment policy revision is current for the decision being made.  
15. No relevant invalidation or conflicting commit has superseded the evaluated state.  
16. The certification decision is authorized and committed through the canonical authority.

If the policy permits bounded exceptions, the result MUST identify the exception authority, affected scope, risk conditions, expiry, and required follow-up. Exceptions MUST NOT be silently represented as full success.

### **17.4 Certification record**

cross\_consumer\_currentness\_certificate:  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_revision: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_digest: REQUIRED  
  registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  residual\_inconsistency\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  issued\_at: REQUIRED  
  validity\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  issuer\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: REQUIRED  
  status: REQUIRED  
  integrity\_proof: REQUIRED

The cross-consumer currentness certificate is a separate artifact from the STEP-147 Stability Certificate. It certifies only the currentness assessment within its stated scope, snapshot, cohort, policy, and validity conditions.

It MUST NOT imply that the underlying Stability Certificate has been reissued or that operational re-entry is authorized.

## **18\. Residual Inconsistency and Closure**

### **18.1 Residual inconsistency classes**

UNDELIVERED\_PUBLICATION  
UNACKNOWLEDGED\_PUBLICATION  
UNVALIDATED\_CONSUMER\_STATE  
STALE\_CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE  
INVALIDATION\_NOT\_PROPAGATED  
INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_UNVERIFIED  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_UNRESOLVED  
IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_UNKNOWN  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_UNVERIFIED  
DEPENDENCY\_SET\_INCOMPLETE  
COHORT\_MEMBERSHIP\_CONFLICT  
REGISTRY\_CONSUMER\_CONFLICT  
EVIDENCE\_TRUST\_UNRESOLVED  
DRIFT\_IMPACT\_UNKNOWN  
OTHER\_UNRESOLVED\_INCONSISTENCY

### **18.2 Closure record**

Every inconsistency MUST have a durable identity, severity, scope, owner or responsible authority, detection evidence, remediation or containment decision, closure criteria, and current status.

Allowed statuses:

OPEN  
TRIAGED  
CONTAINMENT\_PENDING  
CONTAINED  
REMEDIATION\_PENDING  
REMEDIATION\_EXECUTED  
VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
CLOSED  
ACCEPTED\_EXCEPTION  
REOPENED  
UNKNOWN

`CLOSED` requires evidence that the defined closure criteria have been met. `ACCEPTED_EXCEPTION` requires explicit risk-acceptance authority and MUST NOT be confused with technical resolution.

### **18.3 Closure completeness**

A reconciliation run MUST NOT claim full closure while any required inconsistency remains open, unknown, or awaiting mandatory verification.

If a residual inconsistency recurs after closure, the system MUST reopen or create a linked finding according to a deterministic recurrence policy. Prior closure evidence MUST remain immutable and auditable.

## **19\. Durability, Concurrency, Replay, and Recovery**

### **19.1 Canonical commit**

Canonical transitions MUST follow the Kernel's established proposal, validation, authorization, and commit discipline.

COMMIT\_REQUESTED \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED  
COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING \!= COMMIT\_FAILED

When a commit acknowledgment is missing, the system MUST reconcile against canonical History or the authoritative commit record before retrying a transition that could create a duplicate effect.

### **19.2 Concurrent changes**

The assessment MUST be protected against stale snapshots and competing changes to:

* certificate revision or status;  
* cohort membership;  
* invalidation ledger;  
* policy revisions;  
* dependency registry;  
* downstream authorization state;  
* Safe-State enforcement outcomes.

A conflicting relevant commit MUST cause deterministic revalidation, invalidation of the prior assessment, or a blocked outcome as specified by policy.

### **19.3 Replay**

Replay MUST reconstruct the state implied by canonical History without re-performing external actions.

REPLAY \!= REDELIVERY  
REPLAY \!= SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTION  
REPLAY \!= AUTHORIZATION\_REVOCATION\_ACTION  
REPLAY \!= CERTIFICATE\_REISSUANCE  
REPLAY \!= CANONICAL\_MUTATION

If replay encounters missing or contradictory historical data, it MUST expose the discrepancy rather than fabricate a successful transition.

### **19.4 Recovery after partial failure**

Recovery MUST determine which actions were:

* never requested;  
* requested but not committed;  
* committed but not published;  
* published but not delivered;  
* delivered but not acknowledged;  
* acknowledged but not validated;  
* validated but not accepted;  
* accepted but not current;  
* invalidated but not enforced;  
* enforced but not effect-verified;  
* effect-verified but not reconciled.

Recovery MUST resume from the last verifiable state and preserve idempotency. It MUST NOT assume that a failed process implies that no external action occurred.

## **20\. Evidence Manifest and Audit Requirements**

The assessment evidence manifest MUST bind each claim to the evidence supporting it.

Minimum categories:

CERTIFICATE\_ISSUANCE\_EVIDENCE  
CANONICAL\_REGISTRY\_SNAPSHOT  
PUBLICATION\_AND\_DELIVERY\_LEDGER  
CONSUMER\_RECEIPT\_AND\_ACKNOWLEDGMENT  
CONSUMER\_VALIDATION\_EVIDENCE  
CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE\_EVIDENCE  
COHORT\_MEMBERSHIP\_SNAPSHOT  
BASELINE\_AND\_POLICY\_BINDINGS  
DRIFT\_DETECTION\_EVIDENCE  
INVALIDATION\_COMMIT\_EVIDENCE  
INVALIDATION\_PROPAGATION\_EVIDENCE  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_EVIDENCE  
IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_RECONCILIATION  
SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTION\_EVIDENCE  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_VERIFICATION  
RESIDUAL\_INCONSISTENCY\_CLOSURE  
ASSESSMENT\_AND\_CERTIFICATION\_DECISION  
CANONICAL\_COMMIT\_EVIDENCE

Each evidence reference MUST provide sufficient information to verify its identity, integrity, provenance, relevant revision, scope, and relationship to the claim.

Evidence absence MUST be represented explicitly. A list of expected evidence items is not evidence that those items exist, are trustworthy, or have passed validation.

## **21\. Normative Invariants**

The following invariants are mandatory requirements of this proposed contract.

INV-148-001: Consumer acceptance is certificate-revision-bound.  
INV-148-002: Consumer acceptance is certificate-digest-bound.  
INV-148-003: Consumer reports are not canonical registry mutations.  
INV-148-004: Consumer identity includes a validated epoch.  
INV-148-005: Missing acknowledgment never implies currentness.  
INV-148-006: Missing evidence never defaults to successful validation.  
INV-148-007: Required cohort membership cannot be silently reduced.  
INV-148-008: Cohort revisions are immutable assessment inputs.  
INV-148-009: Registry currentness does not imply global consumer currentness.  
INV-148-010: Currentness is bounded by explicit snapshots and cutoff.  
INV-148-011: Relevant drift triggers policy-governed impact evaluation.  
INV-148-012: Invalidation intent does not imply canonical invalidation.  
INV-148-013: Canonical invalidation does not imply propagation completion.  
INV-148-014: Propagation acknowledgment does not replace effect verification.  
INV-148-015: Certificate supersession does not erase prior obligations.  
INV-148-016: Authorization revocation does not imply operation termination.  
INV-148-017: Safe-State execution does not imply effect verification.  
INV-148-018: Unresolved required consumers prohibit full currentness certification.  
INV-148-019: Replay cannot execute external actions or mutate canonical state.  
INV-148-020: Currentness certification does not authorize operational re-entry.

An implementation claiming conformance MUST demonstrate each invariant through executable checks, authoritative state transitions, or a documented verification mechanism appropriate to the invariant.

## **22\. Test Candidates**

All tests below are proposed. They have not been executed.

| Test ID | Test scenario | Required result |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| SC-148-T01 | Correct certificate identity, revision, and digest | Continue to remaining validation gates; no automatic acceptance. |
| SC-148-T02 | Certificate digest mismatch | Reject or quarantine the report; preserve provenance. |
| SC-148-T03 | Consumer epoch mismatch | Reject the report as unauthorized for the current epoch. |
| SC-148-T04 | Missing acknowledgment | Do not infer delivery failure, receipt, acceptance, or currentness. |
| SC-148-T05 | Required consumer is `UNKNOWN` | Block full currentness certification. |
| SC-148-T06 | Optional consumer is unresolved | Apply explicit exception policy and preserve unresolved state. |
| SC-148-T07 | Required consumer silently removed from cohort | Reject the cohort change or require authorized new revision. |
| SC-148-T08 | Consumer report references an older certificate | Preserve historical evidence; reject currentness promotion. |
| SC-148-T09 | Duplicate identical publication event | Process idempotently without duplicate canonical effect. |
| SC-148-T10 | Duplicate event identity with conflicting payload | Detect conflict and prohibit silent overwrite. |
| SC-148-T11 | Invalidation committed but not delivered | Keep invalidation canonical; mark propagation incomplete. |
| SC-148-T12 | Invalidation delivered but no effect evidence | Do not mark invalidation effect verified. |
| SC-148-T13 | New certificate supersedes old certificate | Prevent old revision from being classified current for the superseded scope. |
| SC-148-T14 | Drift detected while impact analysis is incomplete | Preserve uncertainty and apply required precautionary controls. |
| SC-148-T15 | Authorization revoked while operation remains running | Track operation separately; do not claim it stopped. |
| SC-148-T16 | Safe-State command acknowledged but target state unchanged | Do not mark Safe-State effect verified. |
| SC-148-T17 | Safe-State command times out | Preserve unknown outcome and reconcile external effects. |
| SC-148-T18 | Registry changes during assessment | Enforce snapshot consistency or restart/revalidate. |
| SC-148-T19 | Commit acknowledgment missing | Query authoritative History before deciding whether to retry. |
| SC-148-T20 | Replay encounters a historical Safe-State command | Reconstruct state without re-executing the command. |

### **22.1 Additional negative-test requirements**

The test suite SHOULD additionally cover:

* out-of-order invalidation events;  
* concurrent certificate supersession and acceptance;  
* stale policy revisions;  
* revoked evidence-trust keys;  
* incomplete dependency enumeration;  
* a consumer that reports `CURRENT` despite a committed invalidation;  
* reappearance of a previously closed inconsistency;  
* partial enforcement across multiple targets;  
* a consumer incarnation change during retry;  
* a currentness certificate whose evidence manifest has become stale.

A test passes only when its expected result is observed and independently verifiable. A test definition or a successful test-process exit code alone does not prove contract conformance.

## **24\. Acceptance and Verification Gates**

A draft passing these gates is not equivalent to normative adoption, production deployment, or Freeze authorization.

| Gate | Required condition | Failure consequence |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| G148-01 | STEP-147 certificate interface is explicitly bound. | Block integration. |
| G148-02 | Consumer acceptance schema is complete and versioned. | Block acceptance processing. |
| G148-03 | Certificate identity and digest validation are deterministic. | Reject unverified acceptance. |
| G148-04 | Consumer epoch validation is specified. | Reject ambiguous consumer provenance. |
| G148-05 | Cohort membership and revision rules are defined. | Block global assessment. |
| G148-06 | Required, optional, and excluded semantics are enforceable. | Block certification. |
| G148-07 | Canonical and consumer state reconciliation is reproducible. | Mark unresolved discrepancies. |
| G148-08 | Missing acknowledgments preserve uncertainty. | Fail conformance. |
| G148-09 | Drift triggers and impact policies are traceable. | Block impact closure. |
| G148-10 | Invalidation commit and propagation are separately evidenced. | Block propagation closure. |
| G148-11 | Invalidation effect verification is target-specific. | Block affected consumer closure. |
| G148-12 | Downstream authorization dependencies are complete under policy. | Block authorization reconciliation. |
| G148-13 | In-flight operations are separately tracked. | Block affected operational closure. |
| G148-14 | Safe-State authorization and execution are separated. | Block enforcement execution. |
| G148-15 | Safe-State effects are independently verified. | Block enforcement closure. |
| G148-16 | Cross-consumer assessment has a frozen input snapshot. | Abort or rebuild assessment. |
| G148-17 | Certification pass conditions are deterministic. | Block certification. |
| G148-18 | Replay and retry preserve idempotency and do not re-execute effects. | Fail recovery conformance. |
| G148-19 | All mandatory tests and invariant checks have verified results. | No implementation-conformance claim. |
| G148-20 | Separate operational re-entry and Freeze gates remain intact. | Prohibit implicit authorization. |

### **24.1 Evidence required to pass**

Every gate requires a traceable result containing at least:

GATE\_ID  
REQUIREMENT\_REFERENCE  
IMPLEMENTATION\_OR\_SPEC\_REFERENCE  
TEST\_OR\_VERIFICATION\_REFERENCE  
OBSERVED\_RESULT  
EVIDENCE\_DIGEST  
ASSESSOR\_IDENTITY  
ASSESSMENT\_REVISION  
DECISION  
UNRESOLVED\_FINDINGS

A gate MUST NOT be marked `PASS` merely because a document states that the requirement has been implemented. The verification method must match the claim being made.

## **27\. Final Adjudication**

### **27.1 What this contract establishes**

STEP-148 defines the proposed boundary between issuing a Stability Certificate and proving that its consumers continue to hold valid, current, and appropriately enforced state.

The critical control chain is:

STEP-147 STABILITY ASSESSMENT  
  \-\> STABILITY CERTIFICATE  
  \-\> PUBLICATION AND DISTRIBUTION  
  \-\> CONSUMER RECEIPT  
  \-\> CONSUMER VALIDATION  
  \-\> CONSUMER ACCEPTANCE  
  \-\> CONSUMER CURRENTNESS RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> POST-CERTIFICATION DRIFT EVALUATION  
  \-\> INVALIDATION / REVALIDATION  
  \-\> DOWNSTREAM AUTHORIZATION RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> SAFE-STATE EFFECT VERIFICATION, WHEN REQUIRED  
  \-\> CROSS-CONSUMER CURRENTNESS ASSESSMENT  
  \-\> AUTHORIZED CURRENTNESS CERTIFICATION

Each transition remains independently evidenced. The chain is not complete merely because the initial Stability Certificate was issued.

### **27.2 Mandatory safety conclusion**

REGISTRY\_CURRENT  
  DOES NOT PROVE ALL\_CONSUMERS\_CURRENT

CERTIFICATE\_INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
  DOES NOT PROVE ALL\_CONSUMERS\_INVALIDATED

AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED  
  DOES NOT PROVE IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_STOPPED

SAFE\_STATE\_EXECUTED  
  DOES NOT PROVE SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

CROSS\_CONSUMER\_CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFIED  
  DOES NOT PROVE OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED

OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
  DOES NOT PROVE FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

Unknown or incomplete state MUST remain explicit. When a mandatory consumer, authorization dependency, or external effect cannot be verified, the system MUST preserve the discrepancy and apply the applicable restrictive policy.

# **STEP-149 — Cross-Consumer Currentness Certificate Lifecycle / Certification Revocation Propagation / Consumer-State Regression Detection / Re-Certification and Operational Gate Reconciliation Contract**

STEP\_ID: 149  
CONTRACT\_ID: CHRON-CROSS-CONSUMER-CURRENTNESS-LIFECYCLE-149  
VERSION: 0.1.0-draft  
STATUS: DRAFT\_COMPLETE  
NORMATIVE\_STATUS: PROPOSED  
ADOPTION\_STATUS: NOT\_ADOPTED  
IMPLEMENTATION\_STATUS: NOT\_VERIFIED
