CANONICAL\_MUTATION: NONE  
FREEZE\_STATUS: NOT\_AUTHORIZED  
TIMEZONE: Asia/Tokyo

## **0\. Contract Position**

PREDECESSOR:  
  STEP-148  
  STEP-141..147  
  STEP-115..128  
  STEP-135..146

PRIMARY\_OBJECT:  
  Cross-Consumer Currentness Certificate Lifecycle

SECONDARY\_OBJECTS:  
  Certification Revocation Propagation  
  Consumer-State Regression Detection  
  Re-Certification  
  Downstream Authorization Reconciliation  
  Operational Gate Consistency

AUTHORITY:  
  HISTORY \= CANONICAL TRUTH  
  STATE \= fold(HISTORY)  
  PROJECTION \= DERIVED  
  LLM \= OBSERVATION / PROPOSAL ONLY  
  KERNEL \= SOLE CANONICAL COMMIT AUTHORITY

EXECUTION:  
  DESIGN\_ONLY  
  NO\_REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION  
  NO\_IMPLEMENTATION  
  NO\_TEST\_EXECUTION  
  NO\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION

STEP-149 governs the lifecycle of the cross-consumer currentness certificate introduced by STEP-148. It defines how that certificate is issued, registered, invalidated, revoked, superseded, revalidated, and reissued when consumer states regress or their supporting evidence changes.

STEP-148 determines whether a defined consumer cohort is current at a specified snapshot and cutoff. STEP-149 governs the continued validity and use of the resulting currentness certification after that assessment.

Core requirement: a currentness certificate is a bounded, evidence-backed assertion about a particular cohort and state snapshot. Its prior successful issuance MUST NOT be treated as proof that the same conditions still hold after relevant state changes.

## **1\. Purpose**

1. Define a versioned lifecycle for cross-consumer currentness certificates.  
2. Separate certificate issuance, canonical registration, publication, consumer receipt, acceptance, current validity, revocation, and supersession.  
3. Detect consumer-state regressions after certification.  
4. Determine when a regression invalidates the certification, requires reassessment, or permits a constrained outcome.  
5. Propagate currentness-certificate revocation to affected consumers and downstream authorization systems.  
6. Prevent stale certifications from supporting new operations after a mandatory invalidation condition.  
7. Reconcile certificate state, consumer state, authorization state, and in-flight operation state.  
8. Define the evidence required for re-certification and retirement of superseded references.  
9. Preserve uncertainty when revocation or re-certification propagation is incomplete.  
10. Maintain strict separation between currentness certification, operational re-entry authorization, and Freeze.

## **2\. Scope and Non-Goals**

### **2.1 In scope**

* Currentness certificate identity and revision.  
* Certificate registry lifecycle.  
* Certificate validity and revocation conditions.  
* Post-certification state-regression detection.  
* Impact analysis and downstream dependency mapping.  
* Revocation and supersession propagation.  
* Re-certification prerequisites and evidence closure.  
* Stale-reference prevention at authorization and execution boundaries.  
* Cross-consumer reconciliation after regression or re-certification.  
* Audit, replay, retry, and partial-failure behavior.

### **2.2 Non-goals**

STEP-149 does not independently:

* re-run the STEP-147 stability assessment;  
* execute STEP-148's original currentness assessment;  
* issue a replacement Stability Certificate;  
* authorize arbitrary external actions;  
* guarantee that a revoked authorization terminates an already-running operation;  
* repair incomplete consumer reports;  
* define all cryptographic algorithms or transport mechanisms;  
* grant operational re-entry;  
* grant production deployment approval;  
* grant normative adoption or Freeze authority.

Where a required predecessor contract or policy is incomplete, STEP-149 MUST preserve the gap as an explicit dependency rather than silently supplying the missing normative rule.

## **3\. Normative State Distinctions**

The following distinctions are mandatory:

CURRENTNESS\_ASSESSMENT\_PASSED \!= CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATE\_ISSUED  
CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATE\_ISSUED \!= CERTIFICATE\_REGISTERED  
CERTIFICATE\_REGISTERED \!= CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED  
CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED \!= CERTIFICATE\_ACCEPTED\_BY\_ALL\_REQUIRED\_CONSUMERS  
CERTIFICATE\_ACCEPTED\_BY\_ALL\_REQUIRED\_CONSUMERS \!= CERTIFICATE\_REMAINS\_CURRENT

CERTIFICATE\_VALID\_AT\_ISSUANCE \!= CERTIFICATE\_VALID\_NOW  
CERTIFICATE\_EXPIRY \!= CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION\_REQUESTED \!= CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION\_COMMITTED  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION\_COMMITTED \!= REVOCATION\_PROPAGATED  
REVOCATION\_PROPAGATED \!= REVOCATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
REVOCATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED \!= ALL\_REQUIRED\_DEPENDENCIES\_RECONCILED

STATE\_REGRESSION\_DETECTED \!= REGRESSION\_IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
REGRESSION\_IMPACT\_ASSESSED \!= CERTIFICATE\_INVALIDATED  
CERTIFICATE\_INVALIDATED \!= DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED

REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED \!= REASSESSMENT\_COMPLETED  
REASSESSMENT\_PASSED \!= RE-CERTIFICATION\_AUTHORIZED  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_AUTHORIZED \!= RE-CERTIFICATION\_COMMITTED  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_COMMITTED \!= ALL\_CONSUMERS\_RECONCILED

OLD\_CERTIFICATE\_SUPERSEDED \!= OLD\_CERTIFICATE\_HISTORY\_DELETED  
REVOCATION\_ACKNOWLEDGED \!= EXTERNAL\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED \!= IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_STOPPED

RE-CERTIFICATION\_COMPLETED \!= OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

COMMIT\_REQUESTED \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED  
COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING \!= COMMIT\_FAILED  
REPLAY \!= REDELIVERY  
REPLAY \!= CANONICAL\_MUTATION

No implementation or operator may collapse these states into one Boolean where doing so would conceal an unresolved safety condition.

## **4\. Relationship to STEP-148**

STEP-148 produces an assessment and, if independently authorized and committed, a cross-consumer currentness certificate.

STEP-149 consumes that certificate and governs its subsequent lifecycle.

STEP-148:  
  FROZEN COHORT SNAPSHOT  
    \-\> CONSUMER RECONCILIATION  
    \-\> CURRENTNESS ASSESSMENT  
    \-\> AUTHORIZED CERTIFICATION  
    \-\> CURRENTNESS CERTIFICATE

STEP-149:  
  CURRENTNESS CERTIFICATE  
    \-\> REGISTRY LIFECYCLE  
    \-\> CONTINUOUS STATE-CHANGE EVALUATION  
    \-\> REGRESSION DETECTION  
    \-\> IMPACT ASSESSMENT  
    \-\> INVALIDATION / REVOCATION / REASSESSMENT  
    \-\> PROPAGATION AND EFFECT VERIFICATION  
    \-\> RE-CERTIFICATION, IF ELIGIBLE  
    \-\> CROSS-CONSUMER RECONCILIATION

STEP-149 MUST preserve the identity and evidence of the original assessment. Re-certification MUST create a new, linked decision and certificate revision or identity according to the adopted identity policy. It MUST NOT rewrite the original assessment as if the regression had never occurred.

## **5\. Currentness Certificate Identity and Binding**

### **5.1 Required certificate fields**

currentness\_certificate:  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  schema\_id: REQUIRED  
  schema\_revision: REQUIRED

  source\_assessment\_id: REQUIRED  
  source\_assessment\_revision: REQUIRED  
  source\_assessment\_digest: REQUIRED

  stability\_certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  stability\_certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  stability\_certificate\_digest: REQUIRED

  registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_digest: REQUIRED

  baseline\_id: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_revision: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_digest: REQUIRED

  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_revision: REQUIRED  
  policy\_digest: REQUIRED

  dependency\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invalidation\_ledger\_revision: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED

  issued\_at: REQUIRED  
  validity\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  expiry\_conditions: REQUIRED

  issuer\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: REQUIRED  
  integrity\_proof: REQUIRED  
  lifecycle\_status: REQUIRED

### **5.2 Identity rules**

The authoritative schema MUST define:

* whether certificate identity is stable across revisions;  
* the revision ordering domain;  
* the canonical representation used for digest calculation;  
* whether the digest includes lifecycle status or only immutable certificate content;  
* the relationship between expiry and revocation;  
* how issuer identity and authorization are verified;  
* how replacement certificates link to prior certificates.

These rules MUST NOT be inferred from filenames, arrival order, local timestamps, or implementation conventions.

### **5.3 Immutability**

Once committed, the certificate's immutable assessment bindings MUST NOT be overwritten. Lifecycle changes MUST be represented by append-only events or equivalent auditable records.

A status projection MAY show the latest lifecycle state, but MUST remain reconstructible from canonical History. Historical issuance, invalidation, revocation, and supersession evidence MUST be retained according to the governing retention policy.

## **6\. Certificate Lifecycle State Machine**

### **6.1 Required lifecycle states**

DRAFT  
VALIDATION\_PENDING  
VALIDATED  
AUTHORIZATION\_PENDING  
AUTHORIZED  
COMMIT\_PENDING  
REGISTERED  
PUBLICATION\_PENDING  
PUBLISHED  
ACTIVE  
REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
STALE  
INVALIDATION\_PENDING  
INVALIDATED  
REVOCATION\_PENDING  
REVOKED  
SUPERSESSION\_PENDING  
SUPERSEDED  
EXPIRY\_PENDING  
EXPIRED  
RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_PENDING  
RETIRED  
UNKNOWN

These states describe different lifecycle conditions. In particular, `INVALIDATED`, `REVOKED`, `SUPERSEDED`, `EXPIRED`, and `RETIRED` MUST remain distinct.

### **6.2 State-transition requirements**

| Transition | Required evidence or authority |
| ----- | ----- |
| `DRAFT → VALIDATION_PENDING` | Complete candidate record and immutable source references. |
| `VALIDATION_PENDING → VALIDATED` | Schema, integrity, provenance, scope, cohort, policy, and source-assessment checks pass. |
| `VALIDATED → AUTHORIZATION_PENDING` | Validated candidate submitted for the required certification authority decision. |
| `AUTHORIZATION_PENDING → AUTHORIZED` | Explicit authorization bound to the candidate digest and decision scope. |
| `AUTHORIZED → COMMIT_PENDING` | Authorized commit request with idempotency identity. |
| `COMMIT_PENDING → REGISTERED` | Canonical commit completion established through authoritative History. |
| `REGISTERED → PUBLICATION_PENDING` | Publication is required and a durable publication intent exists. |
| `PUBLICATION_PENDING → PUBLISHED` | Publication evidence satisfies the publication contract. |
| `PUBLISHED → ACTIVE` | The certificate meets its activation prerequisites and remains valid under current canonical state. |
| `ACTIVE → REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` | A relevant change requires re-evaluation but does not, by itself, establish the final adjudication. |
| `ACTIVE → STALE` | Current validity can no longer be established from the latest relevant state. |
| `ACTIVE → INVALIDATION_PENDING` | A validated trigger requires an invalidation decision under policy. |
| `INVALIDATION_PENDING → INVALIDATED` | The invalidation decision has been authorized and committed. |
| `INVALIDATED → REVOCATION_PENDING` | Revocation of certificate use is required and its propagation has been initiated. |
| `REVOCATION_PENDING → REVOKED` | The canonical revocation decision has been committed. |
| `ACTIVE → SUPERSESSION_PENDING` | A replacement candidate has been authorized for lifecycle transition under the applicable policy. |
| `SUPERSESSION_PENDING → SUPERSEDED` | The supersession transition is committed with an explicit replacement reference. |
| `ACTIVE → EXPIRY_PENDING` | An expiry condition has been reached or can no longer be proven false. |
| `EXPIRY_PENDING → EXPIRED` | Expiry is established under the authoritative validity policy. |
| Any applicable non-terminal state `→ RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | Canonical state, lifecycle state, or consumer state is inconsistent or unresolved. |
| `RECONCILIATION_PENDING → RE-CERTIFICATION_PENDING` | Re-certification prerequisites have been identified and are being evaluated. |
| Eligible historical state `→ RETIRED` | Retirement criteria are met and the retirement transition is authorized and committed. |

The table does not authorize every transition for every certificate state. The authoritative state machine MUST enumerate legal transitions and prohibit unlisted transitions unless a formally governed amendment adds them.

### **6.3 Terminal and historical states**

`REVOKED`, `SUPERSEDED`, `EXPIRED`, and `RETIRED` MUST NOT be silently transitioned back to `ACTIVE`.

A replacement certificate MUST be issued or registered according to the defined lifecycle. If the system permits correction of an erroneous lifecycle decision, that correction MUST be an explicit, auditable event with independent authorization. It MUST NOT erase the original event.

## **7\. Certificate Registry and Lifecycle Ledger**

### **7.1 Registry record**

The certificate registry MUST expose a versioned projection containing at least:

certificate\_registry\_entry:  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED  
  lifecycle\_status: REQUIRED  
  source\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: REQUIRED  
  current\_registry\_revision: REQUIRED  
  validity\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  last\_lifecycle\_event\_ref: REQUIRED  
  replacement\_certificate\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  revocation\_event\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  reconciliation\_status: REQUIRED

The registry projection MUST be derived from canonical History. A mutable registry row MUST NOT become an independent authority that can contradict History without detection.

### **7.2 Lifecycle event record**

Each lifecycle event MUST bind to:

* event identity and revision;  
* certificate identity, revision, and digest;  
* prior state and proposed next state;  
* triggering event or policy condition;  
* decision authority and authorization evidence;  
* applicable cohort, scope, and dependency snapshot;  
* canonical commit reference;  
* evidence manifest;  
* resulting reconciliation obligations;  
* predecessor and successor lifecycle-event references where applicable.

The event ledger MUST preserve causal links between state changes and their triggering evidence.

### **7.3 Registry reconciliation**

The registry MUST be reconciled against:

1. canonical History;  
2. the source currentness assessment;  
3. committed invalidation and revocation events;  
4. supersession relationships;  
5. expiry and validity conditions;  
6. downstream dependency references;  
7. consumer-reported lifecycle state.

A registry projection that disagrees with History MUST be marked inconsistent and repaired through the authorized projection-recovery process. The projection MUST NOT overwrite canonical History to make the discrepancy disappear.

## **8\. Consumer-State Regression Detection**

### **8.1 Definition**

A consumer-state regression occurs when evidence establishes that a previously accepted or certified condition no longer holds, or when the system can no longer prove that it holds under the current validity policy.

Regression is not limited to an explicit consumer report. It may be detected through canonical lifecycle events, trust-policy changes, invalidation records, dependency changes, operation evidence, or failed currentness checks.

### **8.2 Regression classes**

CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE\_LOST  
CONSUMER\_REVISION\_REGRESSION  
CONSUMER\_EPOCH\_MISMATCH  
CONSUMER\_REPORT\_STALE  
CONSUMER\_STATE\_UNKNOWN  
CERTIFICATE\_DIGEST\_MISMATCH  
CERTIFICATE\_SUPERSESSION  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION  
CERTIFICATE\_EXPIRY  
BASELINE\_CHANGE  
SCOPE\_CHANGE  
POLICY\_CHANGE  
DEPENDENCY\_CHANGE  
EVIDENCE\_INVALIDATION  
EVIDENCE\_TRUST\_REVOCATION  
COHORT\_MEMBERSHIP\_REGRESSION  
INVALIDATION\_PROPAGATION\_GAP  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_REGRESSION  
OPERATION\_BOUNDARY\_STALE\_READ  
SAFE\_STATE\_EFFECT\_REGRESSION  
REGISTRY\_PROJECTION\_DIVERGENCE  
UNCLASSIFIED\_REGRESSION

### **8.3 Regression record**

consumer\_state\_regression:  
  regression\_id: REQUIRED  
  regression\_revision: REQUIRED  
  detected\_at: REQUIRED  
  detector\_identity: REQUIRED  
  detector\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  detector\_policy\_revision: REQUIRED

  certificate\_ref: REQUIRED  
  prior\_currentness\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  affected\_consumer\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED

  regression\_class: REQUIRED  
  last\_verified\_state\_ref: REQUIRED  
  observed\_state\_ref: REQUIRED  
  triggering\_event\_refs: REQUIRED

  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  uncertainty\_class: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_status: REQUIRED  
  resolution\_status: REQUIRED

### **8.4 Regression detection versus adjudication**

The following stages MUST remain separate:

REGRESSION\_CANDIDATE  
  \!= REGRESSION\_VALIDATED  
  \!= REGRESSION\_IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_AUTHORIZED  
  \!= INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED

A report may be a false positive, stale observation, or conflicting observation. The system MUST preserve the candidate and its provenance while determining the appropriate outcome.

However, when policy requires precautionary restriction before full adjudication, the system MUST apply the separately authorized restriction mechanism. Uncertainty MUST NOT become an implicit permission to continue a sensitive operation.

### **8.5 Regression severity**

The implementation MUST use a versioned severity and impact policy. At minimum, it MUST distinguish:

* Informational: a change is observed but does not currently violate certificate conditions.  
* Revalidation required: continued validity depends on additional evidence or evaluation.  
* Currentness lost: the certificate cannot presently support a currentness claim.  
* Mandatory invalidation or revocation: a defined condition requires withdrawal of the certificate's permitted use.  
* Unknown or conflicted: the available evidence cannot determine which condition applies.

These labels do not predetermine the response for every event. The mapping from event class to required action remains a normative policy decision.

## **9\. Regression Impact Analysis**

### **9.1 Required impact graph**

For each validated regression, the system MUST construct or reference a version-bound dependency view covering:

REGRESSION  
  \-\> CURRENTNESS CERTIFICATE  
  \-\> AFFECTED CONSUMERS  
  \-\> DEPENDENT CERTIFICATES  
  \-\> DOWNSTREAM AUTHORIZATIONS  
  \-\> PERMITS / LEASES  
  \-\> IN-FLIGHT OPERATIONS  
  \-\> EXTERNAL EFFECTS  
  \-\> SAFE-STATE OBLIGATIONS  
  \-\> OPERATIONAL GATES

This view MUST include transitive dependencies where the adopted dependency policy requires them.

### **9.2 Completeness**

The impact assessment MUST identify its dependency snapshot and completeness status.

Allowed completeness classifications:

COMPLETE  
INCOMPLETE  
STALE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN

`COMPLETE` requires evidence that the relevant dependency-discovery rules were applied to the specified snapshot. It cannot be inferred from an empty result.

### **9.3 Impact outcomes**

NO\_CERTIFICATE\_IMPACT\_PROVEN  
REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
CURRENTNESS\_LOST  
INVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
REVOCATION\_REQUIRED  
DOWNSTREAM\_REAUTHORIZATION\_REQUIRED  
SAFE\_STATE\_REVIEW\_REQUIRED  
BLOCKED  
UNKNOWN

`NO_CERTIFICATE_IMPACT_PROVEN` is valid only when the assessment demonstrates that the event does not affect the certificate's scope or validity conditions under the current policy.

### **9.4 Incomplete impact**

If the system cannot establish the full set of affected consumers, authorizations, or operations, it MUST record the unresolved set or explicitly record that the set is unknown.

A passing impact assessment MUST NOT be issued merely because no affected item was discovered by an incomplete scan.

## **10\. Invalidation and Revocation Decision Contract**

### **10.1 Decision prerequisites**

Before invalidation or revocation is committed, the decision record MUST bind to:

* the affected certificate and revision;  
* the triggering regression or lifecycle event;  
* the relevant policy revision;  
* the assessed scope and dependency snapshot;  
* the reason and expected consequences;  
* the decision authority;  
* any required emergency or precautionary control;  
* the canonical commit identity;  
* propagation and reconciliation obligations.

### **10.2 Separate invalidation from revocation**

For this contract:

* Invalidation means the certificate no longer satisfies the applicable validity conditions for the affected scope or decision.  
* Revocation means a committed decision withdraws permission to rely on the certificate under the specified scope and effective conditions.  
* Supersession means a replacement certificate has been designated as the operative revision for the relevant scope.  
* Expiry means a defined temporal or condition-based validity limit has been reached.

These meanings MUST be reconciled with the adopted global certificate vocabulary before implementation. If the existing normative corpus assigns different meanings, the conflict MUST be adjudicated rather than resolved by silently redefining terms.

### **10.3 Revocation event**

currentness\_certificate\_revocation:  
  revocation\_event\_id: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_revision: REQUIRED

  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_digest: REQUIRED

  reason\_code: REQUIRED  
  effective\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  trigger\_event\_refs: REQUIRED  
  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_revision: REQUIRED

  decision\_authority: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: REQUIRED

  affected\_cohort\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  downstream\_impact\_ref: REQUIRED

  propagation\_status: REQUIRED  
  effect\_verification\_status: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED

### **10.4 Revocation scope**

Revocation MUST identify its effective scope. If a certificate covers multiple scopes, consumers, or decisions, the system MUST NOT assume that revocation applies identically to every scope unless the policy explicitly requires global revocation.

Conversely, a scoped revocation MUST NOT be used to imply that unaffected scopes remain valid without checking their independent validity conditions.

### **10.5 Revocation commit and acknowledgment**

After a revocation decision is committed, the canonical record MUST remain authoritative even if publication, delivery, or consumer acknowledgment fails.

A missing acknowledgment MUST create a propagation or reconciliation obligation; it MUST NOT roll back the committed revocation or convert the certificate to valid.

If the commit result is unknown because acknowledgment is missing, the system MUST reconcile against authoritative History before deciding whether to retry the commit request.

## **11\. Revocation Propagation and Consumer Enforcement**

### **11.1 Propagation lifecycle**

REVOCATION\_COMMITTED  
  \-\> PROPAGATION\_SCHEDULED  
  \-\> DELIVERY\_PENDING  
  \-\> DELIVERED  
  \-\> CONSUMER\_RECEIVED  
  \-\> CONSUMER\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
  \-\> CONSUMER\_VALIDATION\_PENDING  
  \-\> REVOCATION\_VALIDATED  
  \-\> LOCAL\_INVALIDATION\_PENDING  
  \-\> LOCAL\_INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
  \-\> EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
  \-\> EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
  \-\> RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
  \-\> RECONCILED

Not every consumer architecture must use identical intermediate events. Any omitted stage MUST have an explicit contract mapping demonstrating equivalent evidence and authority.

### **11.2 Per-consumer propagation record**

revocation\_consumer\_record:  
  revocation\_event\_ref: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_id: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_epoch: REQUIRED

  target\_certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  target\_certificate\_digest: REQUIRED

  delivery\_attempt\_refs: REQUIRED  
  receipt\_evidence\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  acknowledgment\_evidence\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  validation\_evidence\_ref: OPTIONAL

  local\_invalidation\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  downstream\_enforcement\_refs: REQUIRED  
  effect\_verification\_refs: REQUIRED

  observed\_consumer\_state: REQUIRED  
  last\_verified\_state\_ref: REQUIRED  
  reconciliation\_status: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_effect\_refs: REQUIRED

The record MUST distinguish missing evidence from negative evidence. For example, the absence of a local invalidation report does not establish that the consumer did not invalidate the certificate; it establishes that the required state has not yet been proven.

### **11.3 Partial propagation**

If propagation fails for any required consumer:

1. The committed revocation remains canonical.  
2. The affected consumer MUST NOT be represented as successfully reconciled.  
3. The system MUST retain its last verified state and uncertainty classification.  
4. Any required precautionary control MUST be applied under its own authorization.  
5. Retry, alternate delivery, or manual intervention MUST be governed by explicit policy.  
6. The global propagation status MUST remain incomplete until closure conditions are met.

### **11.4 Consumer regression after acknowledgment**

A consumer's prior acknowledgment MUST NOT permanently establish revocation completion. If later evidence shows that the consumer again accepts the revoked certificate, restores a stale reference, or authorizes a dependent operation using the revoked revision, the system MUST detect a new regression or reopen the existing finding according to a deterministic recurrence policy.

### **11.5 Revocation closure**

Revocation propagation may be considered closed only when all mandatory consumer effects and downstream obligations have been reconciled under the applicable policy.

REVOCATION\_COMMITTED  
  \!= ALL\_REQUIRED\_CONSUMERS\_REVOKED

ALL\_REQUIRED\_CONSUMERS\_REVOKED  
  \!= ALL\_REQUIRED\_EFFECTS\_VERIFIED

ALL\_REQUIRED\_EFFECTS\_VERIFIED  
  \!= ALL\_DOWNSTREAM\_OPERATIONS\_RECONCILED

An approved exception may permit a constrained result only if the governing policy defines the exception conditions, authority, expiry, residual risk, and required monitoring. An exception MUST NOT be presented as full technical closure.

## **12\. Stale Reference Prevention and Execution-Boundary Enforcement**

### **12.1 Reference validity**

Any component that uses a currentness certificate to make an authorization or execution decision MUST validate the certificate reference against the authoritative current lifecycle state.

The validation MUST cover at least:

* certificate identity, revision, and digest;  
* canonical registry status;  
* revocation, invalidation, supersession, and expiry state;  
* scope and policy compatibility;  
* consumer epoch and relevant dependency snapshot;  
* the validity conditions applicable to the operation;  
* any required currentness or revocation propagation state.

### **12.2 Execution-boundary check**

The check MUST occur at the boundary defined by the applicable authorization contract, close enough to operation initiation to address the relevant stale-state race.

A previously valid decision MUST NOT be assumed valid indefinitely when its supporting certificate can be invalidated or superseded.

The implementation MUST define how it prevents or detects a race between validation and operation start. Possible mechanisms include version-bound permits, leases, fencing tokens, or atomic state checks; this contract does not select one without evidence and governance approval.

### **12.3 Failure behavior**

When the execution-boundary check cannot establish certificate validity, the component MUST apply the policy-defined outcome:

VALID\_AND\_CURRENT \-\> MAY\_PROCEED\_IF\_ALL\_OTHER\_GATES\_PASS  
INVALID\_OR\_REVOKED \-\> DENY  
SUPERSEDED \-\> DENY\_FOR\_SUPERSEDED\_SCOPE  
EXPIRED \-\> DENY\_UNLESS\_EXPLICIT\_POLICY\_SAYS\_OTHERWISE  
STALE \-\> REVALIDATE\_OR\_DENY  
UNKNOWN \-\> APPLY\_DEFINED\_FAIL\_SAFE\_POLICY  
CONFLICTED \-\> APPLY\_DEFINED\_CONFLICT\_POLICY

`MAY_PROCEED` is not a complete authorization decision. All independent capability, policy, permit, operation, and lifecycle gates still apply.

### **12.4 Authorization record**

Each authorization derived from a currentness certificate MUST retain the exact certificate revision used in its decision. If the certificate is later invalidated or revoked, the system MUST be able to identify the affected authorization set under the dependency policy.

## **13\. In-Flight Operation Reconciliation**

Revocation may arrive after an operation has started. The system MUST distinguish the authorization state from the external operation state.

### **13.1 Required operation states**

NOT\_STARTED  
START\_PENDING  
RUNNING  
PAUSE\_REQUESTED  
PAUSE\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
STOP\_REQUESTED  
STOP\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
STOPPED  
COMPENSATION\_PENDING  
COMPENSATION\_EXECUTED  
EFFECTS\_RECONCILED  
UNKNOWN

The actual state vocabulary MUST be reconciled with STEP-146 and existing operation contracts before implementation.

### **13.2 Required response**

For each affected operation, the system MUST determine:

1. Whether the operation started.  
2. Which authorization and certificate revision it used.  
3. Whether the relevant execution boundary was checked.  
4. Whether a stop, pause, or containment action is required.  
5. Whether that action was authorized and requested.  
6. Whether the action executed and its effect was verified.  
7. Whether external side effects remain.  
8. Whether compensation or further reconciliation is required.  
9. Whether the residual risk satisfies the governing closure policy.

### **13.3 Unknown operation outcome**

If the operation's outcome is unknown, the system MUST preserve `UNKNOWN` until evidence resolves it. It MUST NOT infer that a timed-out command had no effect.

The operation ledger MUST retain its original execution evidence and link any stop, compensation, or reconciliation event to the triggering revocation.

### **13.4 Closure criterion**

An affected operation is reconciled only when the required execution state, external effects, and residual-risk disposition are evidenced.

Revoking a certificate or authorization alone does not close the operation's obligations.

## **14\. Re-Certification Eligibility and Evidence**

### **14.1 Re-certification trigger classes**

Re-certification may be considered when:

REGRESSION\_CORRECTED  
REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE\_RENEWED  
BASELINE\_REVALIDATED  
POLICY\_REVALIDATED  
SCOPE\_REVALIDATED  
DEPENDENCY\_SNAPSHOT\_REBUILT  
CONSUMER\_STATE\_RECONCILED  
INVALIDATION\_PROPAGATION\_CLOSED  
TRUST\_STATE\_RESTORED  
COHORT\_MEMBERSHIP\_RECONCILED

These are possible triggers, not automatic eligibility guarantees.

### **14.2 Eligibility predicate**

RECERTIFICATION\_ELIGIBLE :=  
    TRIGGER\_CLASSIFIED  
AND REGRESSION\_IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
AND REQUIRED\_CORRECTIVE\_ACTIONS\_VERIFIED  
AND REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE\_CURRENT  
AND BASELINE\_VALID  
AND SCOPE\_VALID  
AND POLICY\_VALID  
AND DEPENDENCIES\_COMPLETE  
AND COHORT\_SNAPSHOT\_VALID  
AND PRIOR\_REVOCATION\_OBLIGATIONS\_RECONCILED  
AND DOWNSTREAM\_EFFECTS\_ACCOUNTED\_FOR  
AND NO\_BLOCKING\_CONFLICT

Where a condition is not applicable, the governing policy MUST explicitly justify that classification. Missing information MUST NOT be interpreted as not applicable.

### **14.3 Re-certification is a new decision**

Re-certification MUST:

* identify the previous certificate and its lifecycle state;  
* reference the regression and remediation records;  
* bind to a new or revalidated assessment;  
* use a valid cohort and dependency snapshot;  
* use current policy revisions;  
* include a new evidence manifest;  
* undergo the required validation and authorization;  
* create a distinct canonical decision;  
* establish the new certificate's validity conditions;  
* preserve links to all superseded or revoked certificates.

The old certificate MUST remain revoked, invalidated, or superseded as adjudicated. A new certificate MUST NOT retroactively validate operations that relied on an invalid certificate unless a separate, explicitly authorized policy governs that historical question.

### **14.4 Re-certification output**

recertification\_record:  
  recertification\_id: REQUIRED  
  recertification\_revision: REQUIRED

  prior\_certificate\_refs: REQUIRED  
  replacement\_certificate\_ref: OPTIONAL

  regression\_refs: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  previous\_revocation\_refs: REQUIRED

  assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED

  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  eligibility\_result: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  unresolved\_findings: REQUIRED  
  status: REQUIRED

`replacement_certificate_ref` and `canonical_commit_ref` remain absent until their corresponding events are established. The schema MUST NOT fill them with predicted values.

## **15\. Re-Certification Lifecycle and Cross-Consumer Reconciliation**

### **15.1 Re-certification states**

NOT\_REQUIRED  
REQUIRED  
ELIGIBILITY\_PENDING  
ELIGIBLE  
INELIGIBLE  
ASSESSMENT\_PENDING  
ASSESSMENT\_PASSED  
AUTHORIZATION\_PENDING  
AUTHORIZED  
COMMIT\_PENDING  
COMMITTED  
PUBLICATION\_PENDING  
PUBLISHED  
CONSUMER\_RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
CONSUMERS\_RECONCILED  
CURRENTNESS\_REVERIFIED  
CLOSED  
BLOCKED  
UNKNOWN

### **15.2 Required reconciliation**

After a replacement currentness certificate is committed, the system MUST reconcile the new certificate against the frozen cohort and applicable dependency snapshot.

For every required consumer, establish:

* receipt and validation of the replacement certificate, where publication is required;  
* acceptance of the exact replacement revision and digest;  
* rejection or non-use of superseded references;  
* currentness under the new scope, baseline, and policy;  
* completion of mandatory invalidation and downstream authorization obligations;  
* required operation-boundary enforcement;  
* closure or explicit disposition of residual inconsistencies.

A consumer may be reconciled through a policy-approved mechanism other than publication and receipt if the architecture genuinely supports such a mechanism. The alternative MUST provide equivalent authority, integrity, freshness, and audit evidence.

### **15.3 No automatic closure**

`RE-CERTIFICATION_COMMITTED` MUST NOT automatically close:

* old-certificate revocation propagation;  
* stale consumer reference findings;  
* downstream authorization revocation;  
* in-flight operation reconciliation;  
* Safe-State effect verification;  
* residual-risk acceptance;  
* operational re-entry authorization.

Each obligation must be closed by its own evidence-backed condition.

### **15.4 Old reference retirement**

Retirement of an old certificate reference requires proof that it is no longer an active dependency where such retirement is mandatory.

Historical records MUST remain available under the retention policy. Retirement of a reference MUST NOT mean deletion of the historical certificate, revocation decision, or evidence manifest.

If an old reference reappears after retirement, the system MUST detect the recurrence and apply the established reopening or incident-handling policy.

## **16\. Operational Gate Reconciliation**

### **16.1 Independent gates**

STEP-149 supplies lifecycle and currentness evidence to operational gates. It does not replace them.

CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATE\_VALID  
  \!= DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_VALID

DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_VALID  
  \!= EXECUTION\_PERMIT\_VALID

EXECUTION\_PERMIT\_VALID  
  \!= OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED

OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
  \!= OPERATION\_STARTED

OPERATION\_STARTED  
  \!= OPERATION\_COMPLETED

OPERATION\_COMPLETED  
  \!= OPERATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
  \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

### **16.2 Gate input contract**

Every downstream gate consuming a currentness certificate MUST specify:

* accepted certificate types and revisions;  
* mandatory lifecycle states;  
* required freshness and scope checks;  
* required revocation and invalidation checks;  
* required consumer and dependency currentness;  
* behavior for `UNKNOWN`, `STALE`, and `CONFLICTED`;  
* the authorization authority and execution boundary;  
* evidence required to prove the gate decision.

### **16.3 Gate disagreement**

If two downstream gates report inconsistent decisions for the same certificate revision and compatible scope, the system MUST record a gate-consistency finding and apply the governing conflict policy.

It MUST NOT select the more permissive decision simply because that decision arrived later or was produced by a different component.

### **16.4 Operational re-entry remains separate**

Even if re-certification succeeds and all required consumers are reconciled, operational re-entry remains blocked until the applicable re-entry contract independently proves its prerequisites and commits the required authorization.

## **17\. Event Ordering, Durability, and Recovery**

### **17.1 Monotonic lifecycle progression**

The implementation MUST use an authoritative ordering mechanism for certificate lifecycle events. Wall-clock timestamps alone MUST NOT determine whether a revocation, supersession, or re-certification event wins.

A late-arriving event with an earlier causal position MUST be retained and reconciled according to the event-ordering policy; it MUST NOT be discarded merely because a later event is already visible.

### **17.2 Commit ambiguity**

COMMIT\_REQUESTED \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED  
COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING \!= COMMIT\_FAILED

If a commit acknowledgment is missing, the system MUST query or reconcile authoritative History before issuing a potentially duplicate commit.

If the canonical commit cannot be established, the lifecycle state remains unresolved. A derived registry projection cannot independently settle the ambiguity.

### **17.3 Partial failure recovery**

Recovery MUST distinguish:

CANDIDATE\_NOT\_COMMITTED  
CERTIFICATE\_COMMITTED\_NOT\_PUBLISHED  
PUBLICATION\_NOT\_DELIVERED  
DELIVERY\_NOT\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
REVOCATION\_COMMITTED\_NOT\_PROPAGATED  
REVOCATION\_PROPAGATED\_NOT\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED\_OPERATION\_UNKNOWN  
REMEDIATION\_EXECUTED\_NOT\_VERIFIED  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_COMMITTED\_NOT\_RECONCILED  
RECONCILED\_NOT\_GATE\_AUTHORIZED

Recovery MUST continue from the last verifiable stage. It MUST preserve canonical event identity and idempotency, and MUST NOT assume that a process failure means that no external side effect occurred.

### **17.4 Replay**

Replay reconstructs lifecycle state from canonical History. It MUST NOT:

* republish certificates;  
* redeliver revocation messages;  
* revoke external authorizations;  
* stop or compensate operations;  
* issue replacement certificates;  
* perform Safe-State actions;  
* mutate canonical History.

Any action that is deliberately retried after replay is a new, separately authorized execution, not a replay side effect.

## **18\. Evidence Manifest and Auditability**

### **18.1 Required evidence classes**

CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATE\_ISSUANCE  
SOURCE\_ASSESSMENT  
CERTIFICATE\_REGISTRY\_SNAPSHOT  
CERTIFICATE\_LIFECYCLE\_LEDGER  
CONSUMER\_COHORT\_SNAPSHOT  
CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE\_RECORDS  
CONSUMER\_STATE\_REPORTS  
REGRESSION\_DETECTION\_EVIDENCE  
REGRESSION\_IMPACT\_ASSESSMENT  
INVALIDATION\_DECISION  
REVOCATION\_COMMIT  
REVOCATION\_PROPAGATION  
LOCAL\_INVALIDATION\_EFFECTS  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_IMPACT  
IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_RECONCILIATION  
CORRECTIVE\_ACTION\_EVIDENCE  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_ASSESSMENT  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_AUTHORIZATION  
RE-CERTIFICATION\_COMMIT  
REPLACEMENT\_CERTIFICATE  
CROSS\_CONSUMER\_RECONCILIATION  
OPERATIONAL\_GATE\_DECISIONS  
RESIDUAL\_INCONSISTENCY\_CLOSURE

### **18.2 Evidence binding**

Each evidence record MUST identify its provenance, integrity information, relevant revision, scope, timestamp semantics, and relationship to the claim it supports.

Evidence freshness MUST be evaluated under the applicable policy. A valid historical record may establish what happened in the past while being insufficient to establish present validity.

### **18.3 Audit trail**

The audit trail MUST preserve:

* the original certificate and source assessment;  
* every regression candidate and adjudication;  
* invalidation and revocation proposals and decisions;  
* commit and publication evidence;  
* per-consumer propagation and effect results;  
* downstream authorization and operation outcomes;  
* corrective actions and their verification;  
* re-certification decisions;  
* residual exceptions and closure evidence.

No lifecycle transition may delete or overwrite evidence necessary to explain a prior decision.

## **19\. Normative Invariants**

INV-149-001: Certificate identity is bound to an immutable source assessment.  
INV-149-002: Certificate revisions follow an explicit authoritative ordering rule.  
INV-149-003: Registry projections cannot override canonical History.  
INV-149-004: Certificate validity is evaluated against current lifecycle state.  
INV-149-005: Missing evidence never implies continued validity.  
INV-149-006: Consumer-state regression is distinguishable from its adjudication.  
INV-149-007: Regression impact analysis is bound to a versioned dependency snapshot.  
INV-149-008: Incomplete impact discovery cannot establish a complete impact set.  
INV-149-009: Invalidation intent is not canonical invalidation.  
INV-149-010: Canonical revocation persists despite delivery failure.  
INV-149-011: Revocation acknowledgment is not effect verification.  
INV-149-012: Required consumers cannot be silently removed to obtain closure.  
INV-149-013: Stale certificate references cannot support authorization without an explicit valid policy basis.  
INV-149-014: Authorization revocation does not prove in-flight operation termination.  
INV-149-015: Unknown external effects remain unresolved until evidenced.  
INV-149-016: Re-certification does not erase the prior certificate's history.  
INV-149-017: A replacement certificate does not automatically close prior revocation obligations.  
INV-149-018: Re-certification requires current evidence and independent authorization.  
INV-149-019: Replay cannot execute external effects or mutate canonical state.  
INV-149-020: Re-certification cannot implicitly authorize operational re-entry or Freeze.

Every implementation-conformance claim MUST identify the verification evidence for each applicable invariant.

## **20\. Test Candidates**

The following tests are proposals only; no test execution is claimed.

| Test ID | Scenario | Expected result |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| RC-149-T01 | Valid certificate candidate and source assessment | Validation may pass; issuance still requires authorization and commit. |
| RC-149-T02 | Registry status contradicts canonical History | Detect divergence; History remains authoritative. |
| RC-149-T03 | Certificate is valid at issuance but its policy changes | Apply the defined revalidation or invalidation rule. |
| RC-149-T04 | Consumer report reveals a stale certificate revision | Record regression; prohibit unsupported currentness. |
| RC-149-T05 | Regression detected but impact discovery is incomplete | Preserve uncertainty and block unsupported closure. |
| RC-149-T06 | Revocation is committed but delivery fails | Keep revocation canonical; mark propagation incomplete. |
| RC-149-T07 | Revocation acknowledgment arrives without effect evidence | Do not mark the effect verified. |
| RC-149-T08 | Duplicate revocation event is delivered | Process idempotently. |
| RC-149-T09 | Conflicting payloads use the same event identity | Detect conflict; prohibit silent overwrite. |
| RC-149-T10 | Old certificate arrives after its replacement | Preserve historical record; prevent stale currentness promotion. |
| RC-149-T11 | Required consumer is absent from the new cohort | Require authorized cohort revision or block certification. |
| RC-149-T12 | Consumer reuses an old epoch's acceptance report | Reject it for the new epoch. |
| RC-149-T13 | Revoked certificate is used at operation start | Deny under the defined execution-boundary policy. |
| RC-149-T14 | Certificate is revoked after an operation starts | Reconcile the in-flight operation separately. |
| RC-149-T15 | Stop command times out | Keep operation outcome unknown until reconciled. |
| RC-149-T16 | Corrective action is executed but not verified | Block re-certification where verification is mandatory. |
| RC-149-T17 | Re-certification passes while old revocation propagation is incomplete | Keep old propagation obligations open. |
| RC-149-T18 | Commit acknowledgment is missing | Reconcile canonical History before retrying. |
| RC-149-T19 | Replay processes a historical revocation event | Reconstruct state without re-executing revocation. |
| RC-149-T20 | All consumers are reconciled but re-entry gate is absent | Do not infer operational re-entry authorization. |

### **20.1 Additional negative tests**

The conformance suite SHOULD also cover:

* supersession and revocation events racing each other;  
* a replacement certificate with an incompatible scope;  
* revoked trust keys affecting prior acceptance evidence;  
* a consumer reverting to an old revision after successful reconciliation;  
* dependency discovery returning an empty but incomplete result;  
* a stale authorization being reused through a cached decision;  
* Safe-State effects regressing after prior verification;  
* re-certification under a superseded policy;  
* certificate expiry during an active operation;  
* recurrence of a previously closed stale-reference finding.

A test suite is conformant only when its observed results and evidence satisfy the specified oracle. Test presence alone is not proof of correctness.

The following decisions remain open and require resolution in the appropriate normative sources.

| Issue ID | Unresolved decision |
| ----- | ----- |
| OI-149-01 | Stable certificate identity versus revision-specific identity. |
| OI-149-02 | Exact lifecycle event ordering and conflict-resolution rules. |
| OI-149-03 | Canonical serialization and digest calculation rules. |
| OI-149-04 | Meaning and precedence of invalidation, revocation, expiry, and supersession across the corpus. |
| OI-149-05 | Whether expiry is time-based, condition-based, or both. |
| OI-149-06 | Trust authority and procedure for certificate issuer revocation. |
| OI-149-07 | Consumer regression detection frequency and maximum detection latency. |
| OI-149-08 | Required behavior while regression classification is unresolved. |
| OI-149-09 | Dependency graph completeness guarantees and ownership. |
| OI-149-10 | Per-consumer versus global revocation scope. |
| OI-149-11 | Propagation retry, backoff, and escalation policy. |
| OI-149-12 | Required proof for local invalidation effect verification. |
| OI-149-13 | Operation-boundary fencing mechanism and concurrency guarantees. |
| OI-149-14 | Treatment of long-running operations after revocation. |
| OI-149-15 | Conditions for accepting compensating action as closure. |
| OI-149-16 | Re-certification authority and independent-assessor requirements. |
| OI-149-17 | Conditions under which prior evidence may be reused for re-certification. |
| OI-149-18 | Retirement criteria for obsolete certificate references. |
| OI-149-19 | Evidence retention and privacy requirements. |
| OI-149-20 | Exact relationship between currentness certification and operational gate policy. |

An unresolved issue that affects safety, authorization, canonical state, or the meaning of a successful result MUST block the dependent implementation or decision until an authoritative rule is established.

## **22\. Acceptance and Verification Gates**

| Gate | Required condition | Failure consequence |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| G149-01 | Certificate identity and revision rules are explicit. | Block lifecycle implementation. |
| G149-02 | Source assessment and certificate binding is verified. | Reject unbound certificates. |
| G149-03 | Canonical registry projection is reconstructible from History. | Block registry conformance. |
| G149-04 | All lifecycle transitions and prohibited transitions are enumerated. | Block lifecycle adoption. |
| G149-05 | Regression classification is deterministic and evidence-bound. | Block regression closure. |
| G149-06 | Impact assessment exposes completeness and uncertainty. | Block unsupported impact conclusions. |
| G149-07 | Invalidation and revocation semantics are consistent across contracts. | Block integration. |
| G149-08 | Revocation remains canonical after partial delivery failure. | Fail durability conformance. |
| G149-09 | Per-consumer effect verification is defined. | Block propagation closure. |
| G149-10 | Required cohort membership is protected from silent exclusion. | Block global certification. |
| G149-11 | Stale references are checked at the required decision boundary. | Block authorization conformance. |
| G149-12 | In-flight operations remain separately traceable. | Block operation closure. |
| G149-13 | Re-certification eligibility is evidence-backed. | Block re-certification. |
| G149-14 | Replacement certificates preserve links to prior lifecycle records. | Block certificate issuance. |
| G149-15 | Old revocation obligations remain open until their own closure criteria pass. | Block global closure. |
| G149-16 | Replay and retry are idempotent and effect-safe. | Fail recovery conformance. |
| G149-17 | Evidence manifests support reproducible verification. | Block certification. |
| G149-18 | All mandatory invariants have verification evidence. | Prohibit conformance claims. |
| G149-19 | All required test results are recorded and reviewed. | Keep implementation unverified. |
| G149-20 | Re-certification, operational re-entry, and Freeze remain separate gates. | Prohibit implicit authorization. |

### **22.1 Gate result schema**

gate\_result:  
  gate\_id: REQUIRED  
  requirement\_refs: REQUIRED  
  implementation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  verification\_method: REQUIRED  
  test\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  observed\_result: REQUIRED  
  assessor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  assessment\_revision: REQUIRED  
  decision: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_findings: REQUIRED

A gate result MUST reflect observed evidence. It MUST NOT be marked `PASS` merely because a specification describes the intended behavior.

## **25\. Final Adjudication**

### **25.1 Required control chain**

STEP-148 CURRENTNESS CERTIFICATION  
  \-\> CANONICAL CERTIFICATE REGISTRATION  
  \-\> LIFECYCLE AND VALIDITY MONITORING  
  \-\> CONSUMER-STATE REGRESSION DETECTION  
  \-\> REGRESSION IMPACT ASSESSMENT  
  \-\> INVALIDATION / REVOCATION DECISION  
  \-\> CANONICAL COMMIT  
  \-\> REVOCATION PROPAGATION  
  \-\> CONSUMER EFFECT VERIFICATION  
  \-\> DOWNSTREAM AUTHORIZATION RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> IN-FLIGHT OPERATION RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> CORRECTIVE ACTION AND EVIDENCE CLOSURE  
  \-\> RE-CERTIFICATION, IF ELIGIBLE  
  \-\> CROSS-CONSUMER RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> SEPARATE OPERATIONAL GATE EVALUATION

Each stage is independently evidenced. Successful completion of a later stage cannot be inferred from the status of an earlier stage.

### **25.2 Final decision rules**

1. A previously valid certificate MUST NOT remain implicitly current after a relevant regression.  
2. A committed revocation MUST remain authoritative even if distribution is incomplete.  
3. An unverified consumer or external-operation state MUST remain unresolved.  
4. Re-certification MUST create a traceable new decision without erasing prior history.  
5. Re-certification MUST NOT silently close outstanding revocation, operation, or residual-risk obligations.  
6. No certificate lifecycle event may independently grant operational re-entry or Freeze authority.
